distance), and so does the first averaging correction, so the implicit-function step needs $k_e\ge C\sqrt m/\rho_0$
--- Corollary D$'''$.14(ii)'s proviso, and the actual reason for the per-particle norm. That norm is the right one
because the dynamics is a mean-field one --- each particle feels its own position and the class means --- and it
cannot be weakened to a mean-square ($W_2$-type) ball for the nearest-atom leak: a single particle pushed into
another Voronoi cell changes the cluster weights, and the solution converges to the lift of different weights however
small its mean-square displacement --- for $m$ large enough that the shifted weights are admissible. At small $m$
they need not be (at $m = 15$ on the matching prior, one particle moved out of a class of five gives the weights
$(6,5,4)/15$, whose lift is a saddle, numerics below); the solution then splits instead, and either way it does not
converge to $\Gamma_q$. Under the OT leak the plan restores the masses (a transit, Proposition D$'''$.12(v)), and the
basin at the level of the empirical measure is of a different shape: for the averaged loop
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:21]{Theorem D$'''$.21} below proves an $m$-independent energy basin, and its a-posteriori form
covers configurations with a fixed fraction of the particles in other cells (the two sets are not nested, and
D$'''$.18 concerns the real loop).
\end{rmk}
\status{Proved as an adaptation of Theorem J's proof, at Theorem J's level of detail: Steps 1--3 and 5 carry over
with $m$-independent constants by the structural facts (1)--(2), and Steps 4 and 6 are rerun in the norm
$\tnorm{\cdot}$ with (3); the $m$-uniformity rests on the two-sided norm equivalence and on the mean-field structure
of every map built in Steps 1--4. The inputs are those of Theorem J, Corollary D$'''$.14(i), Proposition D$'''$.15,
the margins $\lambda_0$ of $\mathcal{W}$ and the leak's cell and plan margins. It replaces Corollary D$'''$.14(ii)'s
proviso by an $m$-uniform statement and settles the spectral and the basin part of Corollary D$'''$.7's uniformity
question in the per-particle norm. Checked: \code{tests/test_uniform_basin.py}, on the averaged loop at the prior
with centres shifted by $+0.15$ at the matching masses, where the residual is not zero and
$\lambda_{\mathrm{sep}} = 0.80\sigma_0$ (so the per-particle terms that vanish at the matching prior are exercised):
the second-order remainder of the drift is bounded by $C_N\tnorm{u}^2$ with $C_N < 0.1$ for random per-particle
displacements at $m = 6$, $30$, $150$, $600$, not growing with $m$; for a one-particle displacement the displaced
particle's remainder is exactly $N_a + N_b/m$ with $N_a$ the same for every $m$ --- non-zero (above $10^{-5}$) here
and zero (below $10^{-11}$) at the matching prior --- and every other particle's remainder is exactly $O(1/m)$; a
separation perturbation (two particles of one class moved by $\pm v$) moves only those particles at first order, with
the response $(GR_k - \sigma_0I)v$, not the leak's $-\sigma_0v$, at $m = 6$, $60$, $300$; and
$\tnorm{u(s)}\le Ce^{-\mu s}\tnorm{u(0)}$ at $\mu = \min\{\lambda_{\mathrm{coll}},\lambda_{\mathrm{sep}}\}$ itself,
with one constant $C\le1.05$ for $m = 6$, $30$, $150$.}

% Numerics of Theorem D'''.18 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/uniform_basin.md).
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_uniform_basin.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/uniform_basin.md}). The real loop at
$k_e = 25$ ($\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$; RK4 plant with the control and the reference at the stages, Euler
parameters, $\mathrm{d}t = 5\cdot10^{-3}$) for 8000 time units --- 1273 periods, 320 slow units --- with all systems
integrated together. \emph{The rates.} The exponents that test (b) and (c) are Floquet exponents (Part F, by
shooting). At the matching prior the 3-atom orbit's parameter exponents are $2.9355\cdot10^{-4}$,
$3.5579\cdot10^{-4}$, $3.9986\cdot10^{-4}$, \dots{} against $\lambda_j(H_q)/k_e = 2.9521\cdot10^{-4}$,
$3.6155\cdot10^{-4}$, $4.0755\cdot10^{-4}$, \dots{} (0.56--3.22\%), and every ghost exponent is
$2.830\cdot10^{-4} = \sigma_0/k_e$. At the prior with centres shifted by $+0.15$ the parameter exponents are within
0.57--3.15\% of $\lambda_j(H_q)/k_e$ and the ghost exponents equal $(\sigma_0 - \lambda_j(R^G_k))/k_e$ to
$3\cdot10^{-4}$ relative, the slowest $2.2701\cdot10^{-4}$ (class 2) against
$\lambda_{\mathrm{sep}}/k_e = 2.2696\cdot10^{-4}$. \emph{The proof's radius} is small: a sampled lower bound
$C_N\ge0.092$ on the $\tnorm{\cdot}$-Lipschitz constant of $D\langle H\rangle$ gives $\rho\le0.019$ at the matching
prior and $\rho\le0.015$ at the shifted one, so the runs below, at per-particle radii 0.3--2.5, start 31--261 times
outside the proved start radius $\rho/2$: the clean contraction there is evidence that the basin extends to the cell
margins, which the theorem does not claim. (A) The matching prior at the matching masses, where the lift is the exact
representation, a constant orbit: sizes $m = 3$, $15$, $60$, $240$, every particle displaced from its class atom by
$\rho$ in a random direction of the $\Ginv$-unit sphere, the same displacements under both leaks. Inside the cells
($\rho = 0.3$, $1.0$, $2.5 < \Delta_\nu/2 = 3.13$) no particle leaves its cell and the two leaks give identical runs;
$d_\infty = \max_i\min_k\norm{\vartheta_i - \vartheta^0_k}_{\Ginv}$ decays at the fitted rates
$2.69\cdot10^{-4}$--$2.97\cdot10^{-4}$ for $m\ge15$ against $\sigma_0/k_e = 2.83\cdot10^{-4}$ (ratios 0.95--1.05),
with $C(T) := \max_{t\le T}d_\infty(t)e^{\mu t}/d_\infty(0) = 1.03$--$1.29$ at $T = 8000$ --- still growing at the
horizon in the $m = 15$ runs at $\rho = 0.3$ and $1$ (1.16 and 1.27), constant from $T = 2000$ on in the others. The
$m = 3$ runs (one particle per class, no separation modes) are mixtures of collective modes: at $\rho = 1$ the end of
the run is carried by the slowest one and the fitted rate is $2.948\cdot10^{-4}$
($\lambda_{\mathrm{coll}}/k_e = 2.952\cdot10^{-4}$), at $\rho = 2.5$ by the third (amplitudes 0.029, 0.024, 0.086;
fitted $4.06\cdot10^{-4}$ against $4.08\cdot10^{-4}$), at $\rho = 0.3$ by all three ($3.48\cdot10^{-4}$). At
$\rho = 4.5$ some particles start in another cell (1, 3, 13 and 55 for $m = 3$, $15$, $60$, $240$). The nearest-atom
leak ends at other weights --- the counts $(2,0,1)$, $(6,5,4)$, $(23,19,18)$, $(87,70,83)$ --- and not only through
the initial misplacement: at $m = 60$ and $240$ the counts change during the transient (in 3 and 12 records, the last
at $t = 420$ and $285$), and $m = 240$ starts at exactly $q$. Its clouds have not collapsed at the horizon
(within-class rms spreads 0.24--0.66 for $m\ge15$), but $W^2 = 12.3$, $2.81$, $2.30$, $1.92$ is already that of the
lifts of the realised weights for $m = 15$, $60$, $240$ (3.21, 2.35, 1.93): the cross-cluster transport of the weight
mismatch. Two of these weight vectors are inadmissible --- $(6,5,4)/15$ and $(23,19,18)/60$ have splitting margins
$-0.00348$ and $-0.00161$, so their lifts are saddles --- while $(87,70,83)/240$ is admissible ($+0.00167$) and
$(2,0,1)/3$ empties a class (Newton finds no collapsed stationary point near the prior atoms). The OT leak ends at the
counts $q$ at every $m$ (the last count change at $t = 2555$, $2695$, $1610$, $1445$), with $W^2 = 0.010$--$0.23$
still falling at the leak's rate $2\sigma_0$ per slow unit for $m\ge15$ (log-slopes $-0.01411$, $-0.01408$,
$-0.01417$ against $2\sigma_0 = 0.01415$): the transit is over, and what remains is the leak's contraction. The plan
relabels --- 4, 15 and 53 particles end outside the cell of their initial class --- so the OT leak restores the
masses $q$, not the class map $\pi_0$. (C) Remark D$'''$.19's mechanism: the lift of $q$ with one particle of class 0
moved 0.6 of the way to atom 1, a mean-square displacement of 0.311 ($m = 60$) and 0.078 ($m = 240$). Under the
nearest-atom leak $W^2$ grows to 0.79 and 0.20, toward the lifts of the admissible weights $(19,21,20)/60$ and
$(79,81,80)/240$ ($W^2$ 0.8655 and 0.2163); under the OT leak it falls to $3.0\cdot10^{-3}$ and $8.2\cdot10^{-4}$.
(B) A moving orbit: the OT leak toward the prior with centres shifted by $+0.15$ (a non-zero residual, so $\Gamma_q$
moves and the tracking error along it is $2.55\cdot10^{-4}$), masses $q$; sizes 15, 60, 240 started at the lift of
$\Theta^*(q)$ displaced by $\rho = 0.3$, $1.0$ and measured against the lift of the 3-atom loop's trajectory. The
second-half fits $2.18\cdot10^{-4}$--$2.63\cdot10^{-4}$ (ratios 0.96--1.16 to $2.27\cdot10^{-4}$) are mixtures of
separation modes whose gaps exceed the fit window; they are consistent with
$(1 - \kappa)\min\{\lambda_{\mathrm{coll}},\lambda_{\mathrm{sep}}\}/k_e$, and the Floquet exponents above are the
test of the rate. $C(8000) = 1.04$--$1.23$ (still growing at the horizon at $m = 15$, $\rho = 0.3$), and every
$m$-system's tracking error is $2.55$--$2.56\cdot10^{-4}$ at the end.

% Proposition D'''.20 with its Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-15 version; the numbers are
% PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/entropic_leak.md).
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.20}[the entropic OT leak: a soft mass constraint with a critical excess]
Let $\nu = \sum_jq_j\delta_{\vartheta^0_j}$, $\varepsilon > 0$, let $\pi^\varepsilon(\Theta)$ be the entropic plan
from $\mu_\Theta$ to $\nu$ for the cost $\norm{\cdot}^2_{\Ginv}$ --- the unique minimiser of
$\langle C,\pi\rangle + \varepsilon\KL(\pi\mid\mu_\Theta\otimes\nu)$ over the plans with marginals $(1/m,q)$ --- and
$T^\varepsilon_i := m\sum_j\pi^\varepsilon_{ij}\vartheta^0_j$ its barycentric projection, and consider
\begin{equation}
  \frac{\dd\vartheta_i}{\dd s} = G\langle\nabla_\vartheta(r_\Theta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i))\rangle_\rho
  - \sigma_0(\vartheta_i - T^\varepsilon_i).
  \tag{$\mathfrak{A}_m^\varepsilon$}
\end{equation}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(a smooth gradient flow between two leaks)} \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^\varepsilon$)} is the
    $G$-gradient flow of
    $J^\varepsilon_m := m\bar L(\mu_\Theta) + (\sigma_0m/2)\mathrm{OT}_\varepsilon(\mu_\Theta,\nu)$ --- by the
    envelope theorem $\partial\mathrm{OT}_\varepsilon/\partial\vartheta_i = (2/m)\Ginv(\vartheta_i - T^\varepsilon_i)$
    --- and $J^\varepsilon_m$ is real-analytic in $\Theta$ (the dual potentials are analytic in the cost: the
    semi-dual is strictly concave once the gauge $g_1 = 0$ is fixed, and the implicit function theorem applies), so
    there are no plan switches and {\L}ojasiewicz's theorem gives the convergence of every trajectory --- all are
    bounded, $T^\varepsilon_i$ lying in $\operatorname{conv}\supp\nu$ and the fit's force being bounded --- without
    the nonsmooth machinery of Proposition D$'''$.17. As $\varepsilon\to0$, $T^\varepsilon\to\bar T$ wherever the OT
    plan is unique (Proposition D$'''$.12); as $\varepsilon\to\infty$, $\pi^\varepsilon\to\mu_\Theta\otimes\nu$ and
    $T^\varepsilon_i\to\sum_jq_j\vartheta^0_j$: the leak becomes the $\sigma$-modification toward the barycentre of
    $\nu$.
  \item \emph{(collapsed configurations)} At a configuration collapsed by class with weights $w$ the entropic plan is
    class-symmetric, $\pi^\varepsilon_{ij} = \pi^K_{\pi_0(i)j}/n_{\pi_0(i)}$ with $\pi^K$ the $K\times K_0$ entropic
    plan from $\sum_kw_k\delta_{\vartheta_k}$ to $\nu$, so every particle of class $k$ has the class target
    $T_k = \sum_j(\pi^K_{kj}/w_k)\vartheta^0_j$, and the replicated configuration is a critical point of
    $J^\varepsilon_m$ iff its atoms are a critical point of the weighted $K$-atom energy with the leak
    $(\sigma_0/2)\mathrm{OT}_\varepsilon(\sum_kw_k\delta_{\vartheta_k},\nu)$.
  \item \emph{(the separation block)} The Hessian of $J^\varepsilon_m$ at such a configuration splits as in
    Proposition D$'''$.5(i), with separation blocks
    \[
      S^\varepsilon_k = \sigma_0\bigl(I - (2/\varepsilon)\operatorname{Cov}^G_k\bigr) - R^G_k,\qquad
      \operatorname{Cov}^G_k := G^{-1/2}\operatorname{Cov}_{\pi^K(\cdot\mid k)}(\vartheta^0)G^{-1/2},
    \]
    each of multiplicity $n_k - 1$: a zero-sum perturbation of a class leaves the column marginals, hence the dual
    potentials, stationary to first order, and the conditional plan of a particle responds to its own displacement
    through its softmax, which moves its barycentric target by
    $(2/\varepsilon)\operatorname{Cov}_{\pi^K(\cdot\mid k)}(\vartheta^0)\Ginv$ --- a positive feedback against the
    leak's restoring $\sigma_0I$.
  \item \emph{(the critical excess)} When $w = q$ and $\varepsilon\ll\Delta_\nu^2$, the conditional plans charge the
    other atoms with exponentially small mass and $S^\varepsilon_k$ is the OT leak's $\sigma_0I - R^G_k$ up to
    exponentially small terms. Let class $k$ carry an excess, and assume $A_k := \sigma_0I - R^G_k\succ0$ at
    $\Theta^*_\varepsilon(w)$ --- the splitting condition of Proposition D$'''$.5, which fails at large excesses and
    small $\varepsilon$, where the class is a saddle for the fit's own reason. Then
    $S^\varepsilon_k = A_k - (2\sigma_0/\varepsilon)\operatorname{Cov}^G_k$ has a negative eigenvalue iff
    \[
      \varepsilon < 2\sigma_0\lambda_{\max}\bigl(A_k^{-1/2}\operatorname{Cov}^G_kA_k^{-1/2}\bigr),
    \]
    twice $\sigma_0$ times the largest generalised eigenvalue of the conditional covariance relative to $A_k$. $A_k$
    and $\operatorname{Cov}^G_k$ depend on $\varepsilon$ through $\Theta^*_\varepsilon(w)$ and its plan, so the
    critical regularisation $\varepsilon_c$ is the root of $\lambda_{\min}(S^\varepsilon_k) = 0$ along the branch
    $\Theta^*_\varepsilon(w)$. When the conditional plan sends a fraction $p$ to one atom $j$ and $1 - p$ to its own,
    $\operatorname{Cov}^G_k = p(1 - p)\hat\Delta\hat\Delta^\T$ with
    $\hat\Delta := G^{-1/2}(\vartheta^0_j - \vartheta^0_k)$, and the criterion is the rank-one
    $\varepsilon < 2\sigma_0p(1 - p)\hat\Delta^\T A_k^{-1}\hat\Delta$. Below $\varepsilon_c$ the over-weighted
    collapsed class is a saddle and splits; its split-off particles leave toward an atom its conditional plan
    charges, which need not be an under-weighted one, so a split moves the excess but need not reduce it. Above
    $\varepsilon_c$ the class's separation block is positive, and the replicated configuration is a local minimum
    when the collective block and the other classes' blocks are positive as well; the excess is then absorbed by a
    bias of the class atom toward $T_k$.
  \item \emph{(the floor at large $m$)} For fixed $\varepsilon$ the critical excess $\delta_c(\varepsilon)$ is
    positive. At small $\varepsilon$ and $R\approx0$ the critical conditional fraction is
    $p_c(\varepsilon)\approx\varepsilon/(2\norm{\hat\Delta}^2)$, and the plan then sends away exactly the excess, so
    $\delta_c\approx w_kp_c$. The sampled excess of an i.i.d.\ warm start has the standard deviation
    $(q_k(1 - q_k)/m)^{1/2}$, so the entropic leak equalises the cluster masses only down to the critical excess, and
    for $m\gg((1 - q_k)/q_k)(2\norm{\hat\Delta}^2/\varepsilon)^2$ it does not equalise them at all. The masses are
    then conserved, as under the nearest-atom leak, and partly absorbed by the class biases: the stationary measure
    depends on the sample again, and the floor returns as Proposition D$'''$.5's law at the entropic stationary
    point. Only the hard constraint $\varepsilon\to0$ removes the floor --- on the commensurate sizes; on the others
    its $K_0 - 1$ bridge particles leave a floor of order $m^{-2}$ (Proposition D$'''$.12's numerics).
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}
\status{(i) proved (the envelope theorem; the analyticity of the entropic potentials; the limits are the convergence
of the entropic plan to an optimal plan as $\varepsilon\to0$ --- the optimal plan of maximal entropy where it is not
unique~\cite{S45} --- and the product-measure limit as $\varepsilon\to\infty$). (ii) proved (uniqueness of the
entropic plan and the class symmetry). (iii) proved (the first-order stationarity of the potentials under zero-sum
class perturbations and the derivative of the conditional softmax); checked against the finite-difference Hessian of
$J^\varepsilon_m$ at $m = 15$ in \code{tests/test_entropic_leak.py}. (iv) proved under the hypothesis $A_k\succ0$
(the generalised-eigenvalue criterion and its rank-one form); the reading of a split as a transport of the excess is
not part of it. (v) formal (the scaling of the sampled excess and the identification of the dynamics' end state);
numerical below. The structure is that of the critical temperature of deterministic annealing~\cite{S46}, with the
same roles --- the particles as the codevectors, the prior's atoms as the data, $\varepsilon$ as the temperature ---
and twice the largest eigenvalue of the conditional covariance when $R = 0$. New are the two-sided mass constraint,
under which the conditional covariance of a collapsed class is an effect of its mass excess rather than of a data
spread, and the fit's curvature in $A_k$. Checked: \code{tests/test_entropic_leak.py} (the semi-dual Newton plan's
marginals and its agreement with log-domain Sinkhorn; the gradient of $J^\varepsilon_m$ against finite differences;
the two limits at their rates; the replicated configuration critical with a class-symmetric plan, and the
finite-difference Hessian leaving each class's separation subspace invariant and acting there as
$I\otimes(\sigma_0(I - (2/\varepsilon)\operatorname{Cov}^G_k) - R^G_k)$, entrywise; the over-weighted class of
$(6,5,4)/15$, with $A_1\succ0$ checked, unstable at $\varepsilon = 16$, $17$ and stable at $\varepsilon = 18$, $20$,
$64$; every weighted stationary point to a Newton residual of $10^{-9}$).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.20 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/entropic_leak.md).
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_entropic_leak.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/entropic_leak.md}). The instance with
the matching prior (masses $1/3$) at $\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$; the entropic plan by Newton's method on
the semi-dual; every weighted stationary point continued from a converged neighbour and checked to a residual of
$10^{-9}$. \emph{The critical excess.} For $w_\delta = q + \delta(1.5e_1 - 0.5)$ (the excess of class 1 taken from
both other atoms) the branch $\Theta^*_\varepsilon(w_\delta)$, continued in $\varepsilon$ from 256 down to 1
(residuals $\le8.7\cdot10^{-13}$), changes the sign of the over-weighted class's margin once, at
$\varepsilon_c = 5.11$, $13.5$, $23.1$, $65.0$ for $\delta = 0.02$, $0.05$, $0.1$, $0.2$. There $A_1\succ0$
($\lambda_{\min}$ 0.0054, 0.0050, 0.0051, 0.0020), the collective block is positive (0.0073--0.0078) and so are the
other classes' margins: above $\varepsilon_c$ the replicated configuration is a local minimum. At $\varepsilon_c$ the
class sends the fraction $p = 0.057$, $0.15$, $0.33$, $0.57$ of its mass away, about equally to the two
under-weighted atoms (conditional plans $(0.9434,0.0277,0.0289)$, $(0.8485,0.0764,0.0751)$,
$(0.6655,0.1850,0.1496)$, $(0.4257,0.3074,0.2669)$), so $\operatorname{Cov}^G_1$ has rank two; the exact threshold
$2\sigma_0\lambda_{\max}(A_1^{-1/2}\operatorname{Cov}^G_1A_1^{-1/2})$ equals $\varepsilon_c$ to the printed digits,
and the rank-one formula with all of $p$ sent to one atom gives 1.22--1.31 times $\varepsilon_c$ with atom 2 and
0.70--1.22 with atom 3. With the excess taken from atom 3 alone ($w'_\delta = q + \delta(e_1 - e_3)$)
$\varepsilon_c = 6.99$, $14.6$, $24.3$ for $\delta = 0.02$, $0.05$, $0.1$. At fixed $\varepsilon$ the critical
excess is $\delta_c = 0.0042$, $0.0083$, $0.016$, $0.030$, $0.062$, $0.14$, $0.20$ at $\varepsilon = 1$, $2$, $4$,
$8$, $16$, $32$, $64$ ($w_\delta$; 0.0035--0.18 for $w'_\delta$). Up to $\varepsilon = 8$ it equals $w_1p_c$, with
$p_c$ within 12\% ($w_\delta$) and 26\% ($w'_\delta$) of the small-$\varepsilon$ scale
$\varepsilon/(2\norm{\hat\Delta}^2)$, as (v) says; from $\varepsilon = 16$ on the plan no longer sends away exactly
the excess. \emph{Dynamics.} $m = 15$, $60$, $240$ against $M = 510$ from the warm clouds of Proposition
D$'''$.12's numerics, four seeds, $\varepsilon = 1$, $4$, $16$, $64$, 600 slow units, and the $\varepsilon = 4$,
$16$, $64$ runs continued to 6000 slow units. At $\varepsilon = 1$ every system equalises its masses (all sixteen at
$q$ by $s = 600$; fit errors $1.7$--$3.0\cdot10^{-4}$; function-level distances to the reference
$3.7\cdot10^{-9}$--$1.6\cdot10^{-6}$ per seed on $(450,600]$, values at the stopping time, still falling): the OT
leak's behaviour. At $\varepsilon = 4$ the masses move toward $q$ slowly: at $s = 600$ five of the twelve larger
systems sit at unstable weights (margins $-0.00035$ to $-0.00146$ at $\Theta^*_4(w)$, e-folding times
$1/\abs{\text{margin}}$ of 700--2900 slow units, longer than the run). Continued, every system ends at stable
weights (positive margins at its $s = 6000$ weights), the larger ones with residual excesses up to $1/60$, $3/240$
and $7/510$ ($\norm{w - q}_\infty = 0.0167$, $0.0125$, $0.0137$), of the order of the critical excess at
$\varepsilon = 4$ (0.016 for an excess taken from both other atoms, 0.012 from one). At $\varepsilon = 16$ the
over-weighted $(8,4,3)/15$ reaches $(6,6,3)$ within 600 slow units, and in the continuation the three systems at
unstable weights move: $(6,5,4)/15$ to $(5,6,4)$ at $s\approx3700$ --- the split-off particle joins atom 2, which
was at $q$, so the excess is moved, not reduced --- $(6,6,3)/15$ to $(5,6,4)$ as well, and $(25,17,18)/60$ to
$(23,19,18)$; every other system keeps its sampled weights, which are stable. At $\varepsilon = 64$ only
$(8,4,3)/15$ moves (to $(7,5,3)$, at $s\approx1400$). The floors return as Proposition D$'''$.5's law at the
entropic stationary point $\Theta^*_\varepsilon(q)$, 0.55 ($\varepsilon = 16$) and 0.74 ($\varepsilon = 64$) of the
nearest-atom leak's law; the observed floors on the same clouds are 0.23--0.84 ($\varepsilon = 16$) and 0.47--0.91
($\varepsilon = 64$) of the nearest-atom leak's, per seed; at $\varepsilon = 64$ the bias toward the barycentre of
$\nu$ dominates the fit (fit errors 0.12--0.14). The soft constraint thus interpolates in two ways at once: in
$\varepsilon$ between the OT leak and the $\sigma$-modification toward the barycentre, and in $m$ between
equalisation (small $m$, large sampled excesses) and conserved sampled masses with a floor of the nearest-atom kind,
smaller by the factor of the law (large $m$).

% Theorem D'''.21 with its proof, Remark D'''.22, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-16 version; the
% numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/ot_basin.md). The anchor res:thmDppp:21 is the target of the
% \hyperref in Remark D'''.19 (results carry fixed names and no counters, so a plain \ref would print a wrong number).
\begin{thm}{Theorem}{D$'''$.21}[a measure-level basin for the OT leak: a Polyak--{\L}ojasiewicz inequality uniform
in $m$]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:21}%
Let $\nu = \sum_jq_j\delta_{\vartheta^0_j}$ and let $\mu^* = \sum_jq_j\delta_{\vartheta^*_j}$ be a replicated
stationary measure of \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{OT}}$)} --- every atom critical for its class potential,
$\nabla V_L[\mu^*](\vartheta^*_j) + \sigma_0\Ginv(\vartheta^*_j - \vartheta^0_j) = 0$, and the diagonal plan optimal
($\varepsilon < \Delta_\nu/2$ suffices, Proposition D$'''$.12(iii)) --- with residual $r^*$. Put
$\mathcal{J}(\mu) := \bar L(\mu) + (\sigma_0/2)W^2_{\Ginv}(\mu,\nu)$, so that
$J_m^{\mathrm{OT}}(\Theta) = m\mathcal{J}(\mu_\Theta)$, and $\mathcal{J}^* := \mathcal{J}(\mu^*)$. Let $m$ be
commensurate; every vertex optimal plan is then an assignment $\pi$ with $\abs{\pi^{-1}(j)} = mq_j$ (Proposition
D$'''$.12(ii)), $J_m^{\mathrm{OT}} = \min_\pi J^\pi_m$ over these assignments with
$J^\pi_m(\Theta) := m\bar L(\mu_\Theta) + (\sigma_0/2)\sum_i\norm{\vartheta_i - \vartheta^0_{\pi(i)}}^2_{\Ginv}$, and
for a configuration $\Theta$ and such a $\pi$ write
\[
  u_i := \vartheta_i - \vartheta^*_{\pi(i)},\qquad D_\pi := \frac1m\sum_i\norm{u_i}^2_{\Ginv},\qquad
  \Delta\fhat := \fhat_\Theta - \fhat[\mu^*],\qquad E := \mathcal{J}(\mu_\Theta) - \mathcal{J}^*,
\]
and, along the plan segments $S_i := \{\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)} + tu_i : t\in[0,1]\}$,
\[
  \kappa^* := \max_i\sup_{S_i}\lambda_{\max}R^G[\mu^*],\quad
  \kappa^*_- := \max_i\sup_{S_i}\bigl(-\lambda_{\min}R^G[\mu^*]\bigr)_+,\quad
  \kappa^\Theta := \max_i\sup_{S_i}\lambda_{\max}R^G[\mu_\Theta].
\]
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(two identities along the plan segments)} For every assignment $\pi$ with the counts $mq$, optimal or
    not,
    \begin{align}
      J^\pi_m(\Theta)/m - \mathcal{J}^* &= \tfrac12\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 + (\sigma_0/2)D_\pi
      \notag\\
      &\quad- \frac1m\sum_i\int_0^1(1 - t)u_i^\T R[\mu^*](\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)} + tu_i)u_i\dd t,
      \tag{E}\\
      \frac1m\sum_i\langle\nabla_{\vartheta_i}J^\pi_m(\Theta),u_i\rangle &= \norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 + \sigma_0D_\pi
      \notag\\
      &\quad- \frac1m\sum_i\int_0^1u_i^\T\bigl[(1 - t)R[\mu^*] + tR[\mu_\Theta]\bigr](\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)}
      + tu_i)u_i\dd t,
      \tag{P}
    \end{align}
    the norms in $L^2(\rho)$. The counts enter through
    $\Delta\fhat = (1/m)\sum_i[\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i) - \tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)})]$, which
    holds because $\abs{\pi^{-1}(j)} = mq_j$, and through the constant term of the leak. \textup{(E)} holds for every
    coupling of $\mu_\Theta$ with the column masses $q$, fractional plans included ($(1/m)\sum_i$ replaced by
    $\sum_{ij}\gamma_{ij}$); \textup{(P)}, with one test direction per particle, needs an assignment. With counts
    $N\ne mq$ both acquire a defect ---
    $\sum_k(N_k/m - q_k)\bigl(-\langle r^*,\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^*_k)\rangle
    + (\sigma_0/2)\norm{\vartheta^*_k - \vartheta^0_k}^2_{\Ginv}\bigr)$ in \textup{(E)} and
    $-\langle\Delta\fhat,\fhat[\mu_N] - \fhat[\mu^*]\rangle$ in \textup{(P)},
    $\mu_N := \sum_k(N_k/m)\delta_{\vartheta^*_k}$ --- and at incommensurate $m$ no PL inequality relative to
    $\mathcal{J}^*$ can hold: the stationary configurations of Proposition D$'''$.12(iv), with their bridge
    particles, are critical at a positive excess energy.
  \item \emph{(the Polyak--{\L}ojasiewicz inequality)} For every assignment $\pi$ with the counts $mq$,
    $\frac12\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 + ((\sigma_0 - \kappa^*)/2)D_\pi\le J^\pi_m(\Theta)/m - \mathcal{J}^*
    \le\frac12\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 + ((\sigma_0 + \kappa^*_-)/2)D_\pi$ (the lower bound is informative when
    $\kappa^*\le\sigma_0$). If $\pi$ is optimal at $\Theta$, so that the middle term is $E$, and the plan-segment
    margin $\alpha := \sigma_0 - (\kappa^* + \kappa^\Theta)/2$ is positive, then
    \[
      \norm{\nabla J^\pi_m(\Theta)}^2_{G_m}\ge\lambda\cdot mE,\qquad
      \lambda := \alpha\cdot\min\{2, 2\alpha/(\sigma_0 + \kappa^*_-)\}.
    \]
  \item \emph{(a-posteriori decay)} Along every vertex-selection solution of
    \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{OT}}$)} (Section~\ref{sec:thmCprime}, Proposition D$'''$.17(i): an absolutely
    continuous solution that follows an optimal integral plan $\pi(s)$ for almost every $s$; their existence is
    assumed, as in Section~\ref{sec:thmCprime}), $E$ is non-increasing and
    \[
      E(s)\le E(0)\exp\Bigl(-\int_0^s\lambda(\sigma)_+\dd\sigma\Bigr),
    \]
    with $\lambda(\sigma)$ computed at $\Theta(\sigma)$ with its plan ($\lambda_+ := 0$ where $\alpha\le0$); where
    $\kappa^* < \sigma_0$, moreover $W^2(\mu_{\Theta(s)},\mu^*)\le D_{\pi(s)}\le2E(s)/(\sigma_0 - \kappa^*)$ and
    $\norm{\fhat_{\Theta(s)} - \fhat[\mu^*]}^2\le2E(s)$.
  \item \emph{(an a-priori basin, uniform in $m$)} Assume \textup{(H$^*$)}: $\kappa_R[\mu^*](B)\le\sigma_0 - \kappa$
    for some $\kappa > 0$ on a convex set $B$, forward-invariant for \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{OT}}$)} and
    containing the stationary atoms --- for instance the whole parameter space, or the a-priori invariant set
    $N_{R_\Theta}(\operatorname{conv}\supp\nu)$ of Proposition D$'''$.17 ($\bar T_i\in\operatorname{conv}\supp\nu$
    and the fit's force is bounded). Then for every commensurate $m$, $J_m^{\mathrm{OT}}\ge m\mathcal{J}^*$ on
    $B^m$, with equality exactly at the replicated configurations ($\mu^*$ is the unique global minimiser among the
    $m$-point empirical measures in $B$, up to the labelling); and every vertex-selection solution from $B$ with
    $E(0)\le E_0 := \kappa^2/(2(L_2^G)^2)$ satisfies $E(s)\le E(0)e^{-\lambda_0s}$,
    $\lambda_0 := (\kappa/2)\min\{2,\kappa/(\sigma_0 + \sup_B\norm{R^G[\mu^*]})\}$. The constants depend on
    $\kappa$, $\sigma_0$, $L_2^G$ and $r^*$ --- not on $m$.
  \item \emph{(the matching prior; sharpness)} At the matching prior ($r^* = 0$ on $\supp\rho$, $\mu^* = \nu$),
    $R[\mu^*]\equiv0$, \textup{(H$^*$)} holds on the whole parameter space with $\kappa = \sigma_0$,
    $E = \bar L(\mu_\Theta) + (\sigma_0/2)W^2(\mu_\Theta,\nu)$, $\alpha = \sigma_0 - \kappa^\Theta/2$ and
    $\lambda = \alpha\cdot\min\{2, 2\alpha/\sigma_0\}$, which tends to $2\sigma_0$ as $\kappa^\Theta\to0$. The
    constant $2\sigma_0$ is attained by the separations (each particle's displacement within its class decays at
    $\sigma_0$, the separation blocks of Theorem D$'''$.18), so (ii) is sharp there.
  \item \emph{(what the mass constraint removes)} For the nearest-atom leak the assignment is the Voronoi one,
    $j(\cdot)$, whose counts $N$ are conserved inside the cells (Proposition D$'''$.12(i)) and differ from $mq$ in
    general. The derivation of \textup{(E)}--\textup{(P)} breaks at the counts:
    $(1/m)\sum_i\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^*_{j(\vartheta_i)}) = \fhat[\mu_N]$ with
    $\mu_N := \sum_k(N_k/m)\delta_{\vartheta^*_k}$, not $\fhat[\mu^*]$. And the nearest-atom energy has a critical
    point at the replicated configuration of the weights $N/m$ --- a strict local minimum when they are admissible,
    a saddle when a separation margin is negative --- for every count vector $N$ in the domain of the weighted
    stationary point $w\mapsto\Theta^*(w)$ (a neighbourhood of $q$, Proposition D$'''$.5(i)) whose atoms lie in
    their own cells, at a positive excess energy when $N\ne mq$ (under \textup{(H$^*$)}, by (iv)). These spurious
    minima carry the floor of Proposition D$'''$.5. Under the mass constraint they are not critical points; under
    \textup{(H$^*$)} $\mu^*$ is the unique global minimiser in $B$ and the only critical point where $\alpha > 0$ (in
    particular in $\{E\le E_0\}$). Local minima of $J_m^{\mathrm{OT}}$ at a positive excess energy are not excluded:
    by (ii) they need $\kappa^\Theta\ge2\sigma_0 - \kappa^*$ on their segments.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) The stationarity of the atoms reads
$\sigma_0\Ginv(\vartheta^*_j - \vartheta^0_j) = \langle\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^*_j)^\T r^*\rangle_\rho$.
In $J^\pi_m/m - \mathcal{J}^*$ the loss contributes $-\langle r^*,\Delta\fhat\rangle + \frac12\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2$
and the leak
$(\sigma_0/2)D_\pi + (\sigma_0/m)\sum_i\langle u_i,\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)} - \vartheta^0_{\pi(i)}\rangle_{\Ginv}
= (\sigma_0/2)D_\pi + \langle r^*,(1/m)\sum_i\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)})u_i\rangle$.
With the counts, $\Delta\fhat = (1/m)\sum_i[\tilde\Phi(\vartheta_i) - \tilde\Phi(\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)})]$, and Taylor's
formula with integral remainder along $S_i$ turns the two $r^*$-terms into
$-(1/m)\sum_i\int_0^1(1 - t)u_i^\T R[\mu^*](\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)} + tu_i)u_i\dd t$: this is (E). For (P),
$\nabla_{\vartheta_i}J^\pi_m = -\langle\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i)^\T r_\Theta\rangle
+ \sigma_0\Ginv(\vartheta_i - \vartheta^0_{\pi(i)})$ with $r_\Theta = r^* - \Delta\fhat$, and the stationarity gives
$\langle\nabla_{\vartheta_i}J^\pi_m,u_i\rangle = \sigma_0\norm{u_i}^2_{\Ginv}
- \langle r^{*\T}(\nabla\tilde\Phi(\vartheta_i) - \nabla\tilde\Phi(\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)}))u_i\rangle
+ \langle\Delta\fhat^\T\nabla\tilde\Phi(\vartheta_i)u_i\rangle$. The second term is
$-\int_0^1u_i^\T R[\mu^*](\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)} + tu_i)u_i\dd t$. Averaged over $i$, the third is
$\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2$ plus, by Taylor's formula for $\tilde\Phi(\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)})$ around $\vartheta_i$,
$(1/m)\sum_i\int_0^1t\cdot u_i^\T R_{\Delta\fhat}(\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)} + tu_i)u_i\dd t$, where
$R_g := \langle\sum_kg^k\nabla^2\tilde\Phi^k\rangle$ and $R_{\Delta\fhat} = R[\mu^*] - R[\mu_\Theta]$ because $R$ is
linear in the residual. Collecting the $R[\mu^*]$-terms gives (P).

(ii) The $t$-weights $1 - t$ and $t$ integrate to $\frac12$ each. By (E), $E = J^\pi_m/m - \mathcal{J}^*$ lies
between $\frac12\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 + ((\sigma_0 - \kappa^*)/2)D_\pi$ and
$\frac12\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 + ((\sigma_0 + \kappa^*_-)/2)D_\pi$, and by (P),
$Q := (1/m)\sum_i\langle\nabla_{\vartheta_i}J^\pi_m,u_i\rangle\ge\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 + \alpha D_\pi$. Hence
$Q\ge cE$ with $c := \min\{2, 2\alpha/(\sigma_0 + \kappa^*_-)\}$, and $Q\ge\alpha D_\pi$. Cauchy--Schwarz in the
duality of $G$ and $\Ginv$, $mQ\le\norm{\nabla J^\pi_m}_{G_m}(mD_\pi)^{1/2}$, gives
$\norm{\nabla J^\pi_m}^2_{G_m}\ge mQ^2/D_\pi\ge m\alpha Q\ge m\alpha cE$.

(iii) $J_m^{\mathrm{OT}} = \min_{\pi'}J^{\pi'}_m\le J^{\pi(s)}_m$ with equality at $\Theta(s)$. At every $s$ where
$\Theta(\cdot)$ and $s\mapsto J_m^{\mathrm{OT}}(\Theta(s))$ are differentiable --- almost every $s$,
$J_m^{\mathrm{OT}}$ being locally Lipschitz ---
$\dd J_m^{\mathrm{OT}}(\Theta(s))/\dd s\le\langle\nabla J^{\pi(s)}_m,\dot\Theta(s)\rangle
= -\norm{\nabla J^{\pi(s)}_m}^2_{G_m}\le-m\lambda(s)_+E(s)$, and Gr\"onwall's lemma applies. The distance bounds
are (E)'s lower bound and $W^2\le D_\pi$, the assignment being a coupling of $\mu_\Theta$ and $\mu^*$.

(iv) The segments lie in $B$ by convexity, so $\kappa^*\le\sigma_0 - \kappa$, and (E) gives
$E\ge\frac12\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 + (\kappa/2)D_\pi\ge0$, with equality iff $D_\pi = 0$. Since
$\norm{R^G[\mu_\Theta](\vartheta) - R^G[\mu^*](\vartheta)}\le L_2^G\norm{\Delta\fhat}_{L^1(\rho)}
\le L_2^G(2E)^{1/2}$, $\kappa^\Theta\le\kappa^* + L_2^G(2E)^{1/2}$, and
$\alpha\ge\kappa - L_2^G(E/2)^{1/2}\ge\kappa/2$ when $E\le E_0$; $E$ is non-increasing and the solution stays in
$B$ (forward invariance), so this persists, and (iii) gives the rate.

(v) $R[\mu^*] = \langle r^*\nabla^2\tilde\Phi\rangle = 0$. For a separation $u$ at $\mu^*$ (zero class means),
$\Delta\fhat = O(\norm{u}^2)$, $\nabla_{\vartheta_i}J^\pi_m = \sigma_0\Ginv u_i + O(\norm{u}^2)$ and
$E = (\sigma_0/2)D_\pi + O(\norm{u}^3)$, so $\norm{\nabla J^\pi_m}^2_{G_m}/(mE)\to2\sigma_0$.

(vi) With counts $N$ the identity behind $\Delta\fhat$ becomes
$(1/m)\sum_i\tilde\Phi(\vartheta^*_{j(\vartheta_i)}) = \fhat[\mu_N]$; the replicated configuration of the weights
$N/m$ is critical for the nearest-atom energy by Proposition D$'''$.9, a strict local minimum when the weights are
admissible and a saddle when a separation margin is negative (Proposition D$'''$.5), and its excess energy is
positive unless $N = mq$. Under \textup{(H$^*$)} a critical point with $E > 0$ has $\alpha\le0$ by (ii), that is
$\kappa^* + \kappa^\Theta\ge2\sigma_0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{rmk}{Remark}{D$'''$.22}[the mean-field form, propagation of chaos, and the real loop]
The identities and their proof hold verbatim for a probability measure $\mu$ whose optimal plan to $\nu$ is induced by
a map $\pi$ (for instance an absolutely continuous $\mu$: the Laguerre map of Proposition D$'''$.12), with
$(1/m)\sum_i$ replaced by $\int\dd\mu$. So formally the Laguerre-cell flow \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_0^{\mathrm{OT}}$)}
has the same PL inequality, and in the basin both the particle system and the mean-field flow converge to $\mu^*$
exponentially in $W_2$, at the same $m$-independent rate, so
$W_2(\mu_{\Theta(s)},\mu_s)\le W_2(\mu_{\Theta(s)},\mu^*) + W_2(\mu_s,\mu^*)\to0$: for every commensurate $m$ the
particle system and the mean-field flow have the same limit --- no floor, where Theorem D$'''$'s synchronous coupling
kept the transit's mismatch for ever (Proposition D$'''$.12(v)). This is not propagation of chaos uniform in time,
which needs $\sup_s\E W_2(\mu_{\Theta(s)},\mu_s)\to0$ as $m\to\infty$: the bound is not small in $m$ at any fixed
time (an i.i.d.\ warm start lies in the a-priori basin only at astronomically large $m$, numerics below), and
smallness in $m$ during the transit is not claimed; uniform-in-time statements of that kind are known for
entropy-regularised mean-field dynamics under a uniform log-Sobolev inequality~\cite{S50}. The existence of the
mean-field OT flow and its chain rule are not established here. For the real loop: once
$E(s)\le(\sigma_0 - \kappa^*)(\rho/2)^2/(2m)$, every particle is within $\rho/2$ of its atom
($\max_i\norm{u_i}^2\le mD_\pi$), and Theorem D$'''$.18 takes over; on the averaged dynamics this happens after a
time $O(\lambda^{-1}\log m)$. The transit of the real loop is covered only on finite horizons, by averaging with plan
switches (formal).
\end{rmk}
\status{(i), (ii), (iv), (v) proved. (iii) proved for vertex-selection solutions, whose existence is assumed
(Section~\ref{sec:thmCprime}); for this leak a two-particle tie is never attracting --- with
$h := \langle\vartheta_i - \vartheta_{i'},\vartheta^0_k - \vartheta^0_j\rangle_{\Ginv}$,
$J^{\pi_1} - J^{\pi_2} = \sigma_0h$, and on the two sides of the tie
$\dd h/\dd s = \ell - \sigma_0\norm{\vartheta^0_k - \vartheta^0_j}^2_{\Ginv}$ and
$\ell + \sigma_0\norm{\vartheta^0_k - \vartheta^0_j}^2_{\Ginv}$ with the same loss term $\ell$, so no solution
slides along it --- while multi-way ties are not analysed; a Clarke solution in the sense of Proposition D$'''$.17
may stop at a kink-type Clarke-critical point where the active plans' gradients cancel (two particles at one point
between two atoms), which a selection solution leaves. (vi) proved for the critical point; its minimality is
Proposition D$'''$.5's admissibility. Remark D$'''$.22 is formal. The classical case is the leak alone: the metric
slope of $\frac12W^2(\cdot,\nu)$ is $W(\cdot,\nu)$ at every $\mu$, so it satisfies a PL inequality with constant 2
(AGS~\cite[\S10.4]{ags2008}, \cite{S47}); a particle velocity field realises the slope where the optimal plan is a
map. The plan segments are the generalised geodesic from $\mu^*$ to $\mu_\Theta$ based at $\nu$
(AGS~\cite[\S9.2]{ags2008}): the diagonal plan $\nu\to\mu^*$ and the assignment $\nu\to\mu_\Theta$ make
$(1 - t)\vartheta^*_{\pi(i)} + t\vartheta_i$ that curve, along which $\frac12W^2(\cdot,\nu)$ is 1-convex --- the
$(\sigma_0/2)D_\pi$ of (E) --- and the passage from convexity along a curve ending at a point to a slope bound is
AGS's. PL inequalities in Wasserstein space through optimal maps are known for functionals that are not
geodesically convex (barycentre functionals; the distance of uniform point clouds to a density)~\cite{S48}. Here
$\mathcal{J}$ is semiconvex along generalised geodesics only with a negative constant (the loss contributes
$-L_2^G\sup\langle\abs{r}\rangle$), so a global {\L}ojasiewicz argument, as for the $\lambda$-convex functionals
of~\cite{S48}, is unavailable. What is specific is the loss linearised exactly in $\fhat$ with the curvature taken at
the stationary residual, the explicit local constants, and the counts, which make the generalised geodesic a particle
interpolation and the constant independent of $m$. (ii) is sharp at a matching prior and not in general: it bounds
$E$ with $\sigma_0 + \kappa^*_-$ and $Q$ with $(\kappa^* + \kappa^\Theta)/2$ separately (numerics below). Checked:
\code{tests/test_ot_basin.py} ((E) and (P) to $10^{-12}$ relative for random assignments with the counts $mq$ at
both priors, and with other counts the two defects exactly; the PL chain
$\norm{\nabla J}^2/(mE)\ge Q^2/(D_\pi E)\ge\lambda$; the constant $2\sigma_0$ attained by a separation to
$10^{-4}$; from the lift with one particle moved into another cell the margin positive, the particle kept in its
class, $E$ below the a-posteriori bound and decaying at $2\sigma_0$ at the end, while the nearest-atom leak keeps the
floor; the nearest-atom spurious minimum at $(11,9,10)/30$, admissible --- its separation margins and its $K$-atom
Hessian positive --- critical for that leak, not critical for the OT leak, with the PL inequality holding there).}

% Numerics of Theorem D'''.21 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/ot_basin.md).
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_ot_basin.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/ot_basin.md}). The averaged slow loop at
$\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$ from the warm clouds of Proposition D$'''$.12's numerics: sizes $m = 15$,
$60$, $240$, $510$, four seeds, 600 slow units, under both leaks, at the matching prior and at the prior with
centres shifted by $+0.15$. The identities (E) and (P) hold along every run to $6\cdot10^{-13}$ relative. Under the
OT leak the initial optimal plan is never changed (no particle is relabelled between records), and the margin
$\alpha$ is positive from $s_+\le300$ on --- from $s\le25$ in 19 of the 32 runs. From then on the observed ratio
$\norm{\nabla J}^2/(mE)$ is at least $\lambda$ at every record (the smallest ratio$/\lambda$ is 1.00 at the matching
prior and 1.61--1.72 at the shifted one, $Q^2/(D_\pi E)/\lambda$ 1.58--1.69), and $E$ falls from
$4.1\cdot10^{-3}$--$4.0\cdot10^{-2}$ to $8.3\cdot10^{-7}$--$8.1\cdot10^{-6}$ (means over the seeds;
$W^2(\mu_\Theta,\mu^*)$ from 0.99--5.25 to $2.3\cdot10^{-4}$--$2.3\cdot10^{-3}$), below the a-posteriori bound in
every run. At the end the observed ratio is 0.01416--0.01417 at the matching prior, against $2\sigma_0 = 0.01415$ and
the theorem's $\lambda = 0.0140$--$0.0141$: the inequality is sharp. At the shifted prior
$\lambda = 0.0077$--$0.0078$ against an observed 0.0137--0.0139. The local PL constant there is
$2\min\{\lambda_{\mathrm{sep}},\lambda_{\mathrm{coll}}\} = 0.01135$ ($\lambda_{\mathrm{sep}} = 0.00567$), so the
curvature itself costs a factor 0.802 against $2\sigma_0$ and the inequality (ii) another 0.683 (its slack: the
theorem's late $\lambda$ is 0.548 of $2\sigma_0$); the window still mixes the separation modes. \emph{The
nearest-atom contrast.} Under the nearest-atom leak no particle changes cell. At the 22 admissible count vectors
(every run at $m = 240$ and $510$, and $(5,4,6)/15$, $(19,21,20)/60$, $(19,18,23)/60$ at both priors) the runs sit at
the replicated configuration of the sampled weights --- $E(600)$ is 0.02--0.24\% below its energy and still rising
toward it, the classes collapsed (spreads $\le0.006$) --- and that configuration is a critical point of the
nearest-atom energy (drift $\le6\cdot10^{-14}$) and not of the OT energy (drift $2.3$--$4.4\cdot10^{-2}$): the floor
is a spurious minimum. At the other 10, five per prior at $m\le60$ --- $(6,5,4)$, $(6,3,6)$, $(8,4,3)/15$ and
$(24,15,21)$, $(25,17,18)/60$ --- the weights are inadmissible (class-0 separation margins $-0.0004$ to $-0.0061$):
the replicated configuration is a saddle, class 0 splits (spreads 0.04--0.62), and $E$ ends 0.5--16.5\% below it,
still falling. \emph{The a-priori basin.} At the matching prior $\kappa = \sigma_0$ on the whole space and
$E_0 = 2.0\cdot10^{-7}$. At the shifted prior the supremum of $\lambda_{\max}R^G[\mu^*]$ over the whole parameter
space is $6.60\cdot10^{-3} < \sigma_0$, approached at a saturation corner ($a\to a_{\max}$, $s\to s_{\min}$):
\textup{(H$^*$)} holds on the whole space with $\kappa = 4.7\cdot10^{-4}$ --- $\mu^*$ is the unique global
minimiser among all $m$-point empirical measures for every commensurate $m$ --- and $E_0 = 9.1\cdot10^{-10}$ (over
the box of the runs' end configurations, which is not forward-invariant, the supremum is $4.39\cdot10^{-3}$ and
$E_0 = 2.9\cdot10^{-8}$). These basins are tiny: a particle outside its cell is at $\Ginv$-distance at least 3.13
(matching) and 2.95 (shifted) from its class atom, so $\{E\le E_0\}$ holds a fraction $p\le5.8\cdot10^{-6}$
(matching) and $4.4\cdot10^{-7}$ (shifted) of the particles in other cells --- none below $m\approx1.7\cdot10^5$ and
$2.3\cdot10^6$. The runs start at $E(0) = 4.1\cdot10^{-3}$--$4.0\cdot10^{-2}$, far outside, reach the region
$\alpha > 0$ by themselves, and are covered from there a posteriori by (iii). \emph{The measure-level basin.} With a
fraction $p$ of all the particles, taken from class 0, moved 0.6 of the way into cell 1, the plan keeps them in their
class, and $\kappa^\Theta = 0.0096$, $0.019$, $0.029$, $0.048$ for $p = 1/30$, $1/15$, $1/10$, $1/6$ (segment
suprema on 129 points): the margin is $+0.0023$ at $p = 1/30$ and negative from $p = 1/15$ on, the same at $m = 30$
and $m = 300$, which are the same measures. With one particle moved, $E$ decays from $3.3\cdot10^{-3}$ to
$2.5\cdot10^{-5}$ ($m = 30$) and from $2.3\cdot10^{-4}$ to $3.1\cdot10^{-6}$ ($m = 300$), at the rate $2\sigma_0$ at
the end and below $E(0)\exp(-\int\lambda_+)$ with $\lambda$ evaluated at every step ($9.9\cdot10^{-5}$ and
$3.7\cdot10^{-6}$; $\lambda\ge0.00148$ throughout), and the particle returns to its own cell. Under the nearest-atom
leak it stays in cell 1, and $E$ rises toward the spurious minimum of the counts $(9,11,10)/30$ and
$(99,101,100)/300$, both admissible: $5.72\cdot10^{-3}$ and $5.56\cdot10^{-4}$ at $s = 300$, $6.304\cdot10^{-3}$ and
$6.128\cdot10^{-4}$ at $s = 1000$, and the minimum's $6.3126\cdot10^{-3}$ and $6.1328\cdot10^{-4}$ at $s = 3000$.

% Theorem D'''.23 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-18 version, (i)'s pointer to
% Proposition D'''.27 and one sentence of the numerics (Part F) round 19; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/adaptive_prior.md). The anchors res:thmDppp:23 to res:thmDppp:25 are \hyperref targets for later
% results, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The references [S49], [S51] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) and [30] (PARADIGM.md) cited by
% D'''.23 and D'''.24 are the bibitems S49, S51 and R30 of refs.tex.
\begin{thm}{Theorem}{D$'''$.23}[adaptive priors: a learned prior removes the leak's bias, at the rate of the
measure-space excitation]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:23}%
Let $q$ be masses, $m$ commensurate, $\eta\ge0$, and let the prior's atoms $Z = (z_1,\dots,z_{K_0})$ move toward the
barycentres of the particles that the plan assigns to them while the particles follow the OT leak toward
$\nu_Z := \sum_jq_j\delta_{z_j}$:
\begin{equation}
  \begin{gathered}
    \frac{\dd\vartheta_i}{\dd s} = G\langle\nabla_\vartheta(r_\Theta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i))\rangle_\rho
    - \sigma_0(\vartheta_i - z_{\pi(i)}),\qquad
    \frac{\dd z_j}{\dd s} = -\eta(z_j - \hat T_j),\\
    \hat T_j := \frac{1}{mq_j}\sum_{i\in\pi^{-1}(j)}\vartheta_i,
  \end{gathered}
  \tag{$\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{OT},\eta}$}
\end{equation}
$\pi$ an optimal assignment from $\mu_\Theta$ to $\nu_Z$ ($\eta = 0$ is the fixed prior of Proposition D$'''$.12).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(a gradient flow whose replicated stationary points carry no leak bias)} For $\eta > 0$,
    \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{OT},\eta}$)} is the vertex-selection gradient flow of
    $\mathcal{J}(\mu_\Theta,Z) := \bar L(\mu_\Theta) + (\sigma_0/2)W^2_{\Ginv}(\mu_\Theta,\nu_Z)$ in the metric that
    weighs $\vartheta_i$ by $(1/m)\Ginv$ and $z_j$ by $(\sigma_0q_j/\eta)\Ginv$ --- by the envelope theorem
    $\nabla_{z_j}W^2 = 2q_j\Ginv(z_j - \hat T_j)$ --- so $\mathcal{J}$ is non-increasing (under any schedule
    $\eta(s)\ge0$: $\mathcal{J}$ does not involve $\eta$), and bounded solutions converge to Clarke-critical points
    (Proposition D$'''$.17's argument: $\mathcal{J}$ is a minimum over the vertices of definable smooth functions of
    $(\Theta,Z)$). Boundedness is now a hypothesis (Proposition D$'''$.27 restores it by projecting the prior onto a
    compact convex set). Proposition D$'''$.17's a-priori bound rested on the fixed prior's support, while the leak
    toward an adaptive prior is an internal force between a class and its atom --- with the plan held, the leak
    cancels from
    $\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}[(1/m)\sum_{i\in\pi^{-1}(j)}\vartheta_i + (\sigma_0q_j/\eta)z_j]
    = (1/m)\sum_{i\in\pi^{-1}(j)}G\langle r\nabla\tilde\Phi(\vartheta_i)\rangle$ --- and solutions can leave every
    compact set (numerics, Part G(a) and (b)), even from a fit that the network attains exactly. At a stationary
    point $z_j = \hat T_j$, and each particle satisfies
    $G\langle r\nabla\tilde\Phi(\vartheta_i)\rangle = \sigma_0(\vartheta_i - \hat T_{\pi(i)})$: the learning forces
    cancel over each class, but a particle may be held off its class barycentre by the leak. A replicated
    configuration --- the particles of each class $\pi^{-1}(j)$ at one point $\vartheta_j$ --- is therefore stationary
    with $Z$ iff $z_j = \vartheta_j$ and $\nabla V_L[\sum_lq_l\delta_{\vartheta_l}](\vartheta_j) = 0$ for every $j$:
    the leak force vanishes, and the stationary replicated configurations are the critical points of the
    unregularised weighted $K_0$-atom loss. (The classes are the plan's: labelled by the atoms of a given
    configuration this reads $z_{\sigma(k)} = \vartheta_k$ for the plan's class-to-atom bijection $\sigma$, and at an
    exact representation a prior permuted among atoms of equal mass is stationary; a permutation between atoms of
    different masses is not, since a replicated configuration with $W^2 = 0$ needs $q_{\sigma(k)} = q_k$.)
    Non-replicated stationary points --- a class held apart against the loss by the leak toward its barycentre ---
    exist (a saddle on the instance), and carry the $K_0$-cluster bias of (iv)'s regulariser (numerics, Part G(a)).
    The stationary equations do not involve the prior's initial position: an exact representation with the weights
    $q$, with the prior at its atoms, is stationary and a global minimum ($\mathcal{J} = 0$ there); which stationary
    point is reached does depend on it ((vi)).
  \item \emph{(the linearisation at an exact representation)} At an exact representation
    $\mu^* = \sum_kq_k\delta_{\vartheta^*_k}$ ($r^* = 0$) with $Z = (\vartheta^*_k)$, the linearisation of
    \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{OT},\eta}$)} splits, for every $m$, into the separations of each class, which
    decay at $\sigma_0$, and the collective pairs $(\bar u,\zeta)$ of class means and prior offsets. In the whitened
    weighted coordinates $y_k := \sqrt{q_k}G^{-1/2}\bar u_k$, $x_k := \sqrt{q_k}G^{-1/2}\zeta_k$ it reads
    $\dot y = -H_Ly - \sigma_0(y - x)$, $\dot x = -\eta(x - y)$, with the Gauss--Newton block
    $H_L := [\sqrt{q_kq_l}G^{1/2}\langle\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^*_k)^\T
    \nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^*_l)\rangle_\rho G^{1/2}]_{kl}\succeq0$ (eigenvalues
    $h_1\le\dots\le h_{pK_0}$). On its eigenvectors every pair evolves by
    $\bigl[\begin{smallmatrix}-(h_j + \sigma_0) & \sigma_0\\ \eta & -\eta\end{smallmatrix}\bigr]$, whose rates
    $\lambda_j^\pm$ are the roots of $\lambda^2 - (h_j + \sigma_0 + \eta)\lambda + \eta h_j = 0$; for $h_j,\eta > 0$
    the slower one satisfies
    \[
      \eta h_j/(h_j + \sigma_0 + \eta) < \lambda_j^- < \min\{h_j,\eta\},
    \]
    the lower bound being tight as $\eta\to0$; $\lambda_j^- = 0$ if $h_j\eta = 0$ and $\lambda_j^- = h_j$ at
    $\eta = \infty$; and $\lambda^-$ increases with $h$
    ($\dd\lambda^-/\dd h = (\eta - \lambda^-)/(\lambda^+ - \lambda^-) > 0$), so the slowest collective rate is
    $\lambda_1^-$. So the adaptive prior's convergence in mode $j$ is never faster than the loss's own curvature
    $h_j$, while a fixed prior's is $h_j + \sigma_0$; in an unexcited mode ($h_j = 0$) $\lambda_j^- = 0$, and the
    network and the prior drift together, as an adaptive law without a leak does without persistent excitation. The
    pair matrix is self-adjoint in the metric of (i) (in the coordinates $(y_j,(\sigma_0/\eta)^{1/2}x_j)$ it is
    symmetric, with off-diagonal $(\sigma_0\eta)^{1/2}$).
  \item \emph{(the bias it removes)} With a fixed prior near an exact representation, the stationary point is, to
    first order in the prior's error $x^0$, $y^* = \sigma_0(H_L + \sigma_0I)^{-1}x^0$: mode $j$ keeps the fraction
    $\sigma_0/(h_j + \sigma_0)$ of the error. The leak buys the rate $h_j + \sigma_0$ at the price of that fraction,
    and the adaptive prior removes it at the rate $\lambda_j^-$. Weakly excited modes ($h_j\ll\sigma_0$) keep almost
    all of the prior's error under a fixed prior and lose it slowly under an adaptive one; the fit,
    $\frac12\sum_jh_jy_j^2$ to second order, weighs them by $h_j$, so the fit is restored faster than the parameters.
    The spectrum $\{h_j\}$ is the measure-space excitation level of the reference at $\mu^*$
    (Section~\ref{sec:program}, branch 7).
  \item \emph{(infinitely fast priors: a capacity-constrained $K_0$-means regulariser)} Formally, as $\eta\to\infty$
    the prior follows the class barycentres, $z_j = \hat T_j$, and \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{OT},\eta}$)}
    becomes the gradient flow of $\bar L(\mu_\Theta) + (\sigma_0/2)\mathcal{Q}_q(\mu_\Theta)$, with
    $\mathcal{Q}_q(\mu) := \min_ZW^2(\mu,\nu_Z)$ the capacity-constrained $K_0$-means distortion of $\mu$ ---
    along the plan the flow carries, which is optimal for its own barycentres (Lloyd-consistent) and need not be
    jointly optimal: the flow follows the selected piece of $\mathcal{Q}_q$, and $\mathcal{Q}_q$ itself need not
    decrease (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:27]{Proposition D$'''$.27}(ii)) ---: a regulariser that pulls the network
    toward $K_0$ clusters of the prescribed masses without biasing where they sit.
    The separations keep the rate $\sigma_0$, and mode $j$ of the class means relaxes at $h_j$. It does bias how many
    there are: a target that the network fits exactly only with a class split is fitted with $K_0$ clusters
    (numerics, Part G), so $K_0$ remains a design input.
  \item \emph{(uniformity in $m$)} The blocks of (ii) and their rates do not depend on $m$. In the norm
    \[
      \tnorm{(u,\zeta)}_\eta := \max\Bigl\{\Bigl(\sum_kq_k\bigl(\norm{\bar u_k}^2_{\Ginv}
      + (\sigma_0/\eta)\norm{\zeta_k}^2_{\Ginv}\bigr)\Bigr)^{1/2},\ \max_i\norm{u_i^\perp}_{\Ginv}\Bigr\},
    \]
    the per-particle norm of Theorem D$'''$.18 with the prior's offsets added in the metric of (i), the linearisation
    acts on the separations as $-\sigma_0I$ and is self-adjoint on the collective part with the eigenvalues
    $-\lambda_j^\pm$; the prior is driven by the class barycentres, which are linear in the class means while the
    plan is diagonal, so Theorem D$'''$.18's facts (1)--(2) hold for the joint drift with $m$-independent constants,
    and for $0 < \eta < \infty$ the averaged loop converges, for every commensurate $m$, in the
    $\tnorm{\cdot}_\eta$-ball of radius
    \begin{gather*}
      \rho(\eta) := \min\Bigl\{\lambda_0(\eta)/(4C_N),\
      \mathrm{margin}/\bigl(C[1 + q_{\min}^{-1/2} + (\eta/(\sigma_0q_{\min}))^{1/2}]\bigr)\Bigr\},\\
      \lambda_0(\eta) := \min\{\sigma_0,\lambda_1^-(\eta)\},
    \end{gather*}
    at the rate $(1 - \kappa)\lambda_0(\eta)$ --- $C_N$ the Lipschitz constant of the joint drift's Jacobian in the
    $\tnorm{\cdot}_\eta$-operator norm (the prior's part of the drift is linear, so it is the particles'),
    $\mathrm{margin}$ the plan margin of Proposition D$'''$.12(iii) at the exact representation, $C$ as in Theorem
    D$'''$.18: the ball reaches the per-particle distance $(1 + q_{\min}^{-1/2})\rho$ and the prior's offsets
    $\norm{\zeta_k}_{\Ginv}\le\rho(\eta/(\sigma_0q_k))^{1/2}$, and the plan stays diagonal while both are within the
    margin. At $\eta = \infty$ the prior is the class barycentres ($\zeta = \bar u$), the norm is Theorem
    D$'''$.18's, $\lambda_0 = \min\{\sigma_0,h_1\}$, and the margin term is
    $\mathrm{margin}/(C(1 + 2q_{\min}^{-1/2}))$; for large finite $\eta$ the bound degenerates like $\eta^{-1/2}$,
    because this norm does not use the prior's relaxation onto the class means at the rate $\eta$ (a singular
    perturbation). As $\eta\to0$, $\lambda_1^- < \eta$ and the rate and the radius vanish with it; at $\eta = 0$ the
    prior is a parameter, and the statement is Theorem D$'''$.18's contraction for the fixed prior.
  \item \emph{(the transit: when the prior may start)} From a sample whose cluster masses differ from $q$, the plan's
    classes contain particles of other clusters until the transit of Proposition D$'''$.12 ends, and their
    barycentres $\hat T_j$ are pulled toward those clusters: an adaptive prior is dragged along, and afterwards
    relaxes back only at the rates $\lambda_j^-$. At $\eta = \infty$ the collective modes keep no leak curvature at
    all, and the drag can carry the class atoms onto a plateau of the unregularised loss, which the flow then crosses
    at that loss's own curvature. Freezing the prior while mass is in transit --- $\eta = 0$ as long as some particle
    is assigned to an atom that is not its nearest --- keeps the prior out of the drag; $\mathcal{J}$ stays
    non-increasing, as under any schedule of $\eta$, and at $\eta = \infty$ the opening is a jump of $Z$ to the class
    barycentres, which lowers $\mathcal{J}$. But the adaptive prior forgets a perturbation of mode $j$ only at
    $\lambda_j^- < h_j$, so whatever the transient leaves in the weakly excited modes when the prior starts is kept
    for a time of order $1/\lambda_1^-$. When the parameters, not only the fit, are wanted at a given horizon, the
    adaptation should therefore start from the fixed-prior stationary point --- after the transit, and after the
    fixed-prior dynamics has settled, which it does at the fixed prior's rates (of order $\sigma_0$ and faster) ---
    and then (ii) and (iii) apply with the prior's error as the only initial displacement. The wait is a delay, which
    the fit error does not always repay (numerics).
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) $W^2(\mu_\Theta,\nu_Z) = \min_\gamma\sum_{ij}\gamma_{ij}\norm{\vartheta_i - z_j}^2_{\Ginv}$; where the optimal plan
is unique, $\nabla_{z_j}W^2 = \sum_i\gamma_{ij}2\Ginv(z_j - \vartheta_i) = 2q_j\Ginv(z_j - \hat T_j)$, so
$\dd z_j/\dd s = -(\eta/(\sigma_0q_j))G\nabla_{z_j}\mathcal{J}$ and
$\dd\vartheta_i/\dd s = -mG\nabla_{\vartheta_i}\mathcal{J}$ (Proposition D$'''$.12); the vertex-selection argument and
the convergence are Proposition D$'''$.17's with $(\Theta,Z)$ in place of $\Theta$, for solutions that stay bounded.
With the plan held,
$(1/m)\sum_{i\in\pi^{-1}(j)}\sigma_0(\vartheta_i - z_j) = \sigma_0q_j(\hat T_j - z_j) = (\sigma_0q_j/\eta)\dot z_j$,
which is the conservation law. $\dot z = 0$ forces $z_j = \hat T_j$ for every $j$, and then $\dot\vartheta_i = 0$
reads $G\langle r\nabla\tilde\Phi(\vartheta_i)\rangle = \sigma_0(\vartheta_i - \hat T_{\pi(i)})$. At a replicated
configuration $\hat T_j$ is the class point $\vartheta_j$, so the leak term vanishes and stationarity is
$z_j = \vartheta_j$ and $\nabla V_L(\vartheta_j) = 0$. At an exact representation $\bar L = 0$ and
$W^2(\mu^*,\nu_{Z^*}) = 0$. (ii) At $r^* = 0$,
$-G\nabla V_L[\mu](\vartheta_i) = -G\langle\Delta\fhat^\T\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i)\rangle$ with
$\Delta\fhat = \sum_lq_l\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^*_l)\bar u_l + O(\norm{u}^2)$ (the separations
enter at second order, Theorem D$'''$.18's fact (1)), so a separation evolves by $-\sigma_0u^\perp$ and the class
means by $\dot{\bar u}_k = -G\sum_l\langle\nabla\tilde\Phi_k^\T\nabla\tilde\Phi_l\rangle q_l\bar u_l
- \sigma_0(\bar u_k - \zeta_k)$, with $\dot\zeta_k = -\eta(\zeta_k - \bar u_k)$ because
$\hat T_k - \vartheta^*_k = \bar u_k$; the coordinates $y$, $x$ symmetrise it. The eigenvalues of the pair matrix are
$-\lambda$ with $\lambda^2 - (h + \sigma_0 + \eta)\lambda + \eta h = 0$. Their product is $\eta h$ and their sum
$h + \sigma_0 + \eta$; for $h,\eta > 0$ both roots are positive, so $\lambda^+ < h + \sigma_0 + \eta$ and
$\lambda^- = \eta h/\lambda^+ > \eta h/(h + \sigma_0 + \eta)$; the polynomial is $-\sigma_0h < 0$ at $\lambda = h$ and
$-\sigma_0\eta < 0$ at $\lambda = \eta$, so both lie strictly between the roots; and differentiating the polynomial
in $h$ at $\lambda^-$ gives $\dd\lambda^-/\dd h = (\eta - \lambda^-)/(\lambda^+ - \lambda^-) > 0$. (iii) The
stationary equations of the fixed prior, linearised, read $0 = -H_Ly - \sigma_0(y - x^0)$;
$\bar L = \frac12\norm{\Delta\fhat}^2 = \frac12y^\T H_Ly + O(\norm{y}^3)$. (iv) For a fixed assignment the minimiser
of $W^2$ over $Z$ is the class barycentres; by the envelope theorem the particles' drift of
$\bar L + (\sigma_0/2)\mathcal{Q}_q$ is $-G\nabla V_L - \sigma_0(\vartheta_i - \hat T_{\pi(i)})$, the $\eta\to\infty$
limit of \textup{($\mathfrak{A}_m^{\mathrm{OT},\eta}$)} (formal: a singular perturbation in which $Z$ is the fast
variable). (v) (sketch) The symmetrisation of (ii) makes the collective part self-adjoint in $\tnorm{\cdot}_\eta$,
with the eigenvalues $-\lambda_j^\pm$, and the separations decouple at $-\sigma_0$; the rest is Theorem D$'''$.18's
contraction argument (its fact (3) with this norm) for the averaged loop, the diagonal plan being locally constant at
commensurate $m$ (Proposition D$'''$.12(iii)) inside the ball, whose per-particle and prior extents are as stated.
The constants $\kappa$, $C$, $C_N$ and the margin are not derived. (vi) $\mathcal{J}$ does not involve $\eta$, so it
is non-increasing for any schedule $\eta(s)\ge0$, the gates' included; at $\eta = \infty$ the opening sets $Z$ to the
class barycentres of the current plan, the minimiser of $\mathcal{J}$ over $Z$ for that plan, so $\mathcal{J}$ drops
there.
\end{proof}
\status{(i)--(iii) proved, (i) for the solutions that stay bounded (a hypothesis now); (iv) formal; (v) sketched, as
an adaptation of Theorem D$'''$.18's contraction argument for the averaged loop in the norm $\tnorm{\cdot}_\eta$ ---
the structure (the decoupled separations, the self-adjoint collective pairs, the $m$-independent blocks) verified, the
radius stated, its constants not derived; (vi) the monotonicity of $\mathcal{J}$ under the gates proved (it holds
under any schedule of $\eta$), the drag, the memory of the weak modes and the remedy numerical (below). The prior
that tracks the network is the continuous-time form of the proximal point method, or iterated Tikhonov
regularisation --- the leak toward a moving centre --- and the coupled flow of the particles and a centre drawn to
their mean is elastic averaging; the fast limit is the $K$-means form of soft weight-sharing, a regulariser of network
compression \cite{S49}, here with the masses fixed (capacity-constrained, \cite{S19}). The per-mode formulas are those of
the quadratic analyses: $\sigma_0/(h_j + \sigma_0)$ is the complement $1 - h_j/(h_j + \sigma_0)$ of the Tikhonov
filter factor in the Gauss--Newton eigenbasis (the residual factor: the fraction of the prior's error that the
regularised solution keeps), iterated Tikhonov contracts mode $j$ by it per step (in continuous time, the gradient
flow of the Moreau envelope, whose curvature $h_j\sigma_0/(h_j + \sigma_0)$ gives the slow-prior limit
$\eta h_j/(h_j + \sigma_0)$ of $\lambda_j^-$), and the pair matrix is the continuous-time form of elastic averaging's
worker--centre map on a quadratic \cite{S49}. New are their transfer to the mass-constrained particle system --- $H_L$
identified as the measure-space excitation spectrum, and the separations decoupled at $\sigma_0$, which make the
trade-off between the fixed prior's bias and the adaptive prior's rate hold uniformly in $m$ --- and the transit's
drag with its gate. The limits of the bias removal are the precedents' own properties in this form: the lost
a-priori bound is the proximal point method's behaviour without a minimiser (its iterates are bounded iff a zero
exists \cite{S49}, cited from memory), and the $K_0$-cluster bias is the quantisation error of soft weight-sharing's hard
limit \cite{S49}; specific here are their particle and mass-constrained form, and the run-off from a fit that the network
attains exactly (Part G(a)). Checked: \code{tests/test_adaptive_prior.py} (the joint drift against finite differences
of $\mathcal{J}$ in the stated metric; the exact representation stationary with the prior at its atoms and the fixed
prior's stationary point not; the spectrum of the linearisation at $m = 6$ equal to the separation rate $\sigma_0$
and the pair rates $\lambda_j^\pm$ for two $\eta$, and the linearisation self-adjoint in the gradient-flow metric;
the fixed prior's bias at a smaller shift to the first-order formula; the transit gate frozen while mass is in
transit and the settled gate opening only when the fixed-prior drift has fallen below its tolerance, with
$\mathcal{J}$ non-increasing in both; the limits of (i): a non-replicated stationary saddle held by the leak, a prior
permuted among atoms of equal mass stationary at the exact representation, and the adaptive prior's run-off --- the
force on the amplitude tending to the positive Karush--Kuhn--Tucker multiplier of the loss's minimum on the face
$a = a_{\max}$, where a bounded limit would need it to vanish, and the run-off law
$(a_{\max} - a)s\to a_{\max}^2(\sigma_0 + \eta)/(4\eta G_\alpha F_\infty)$ of the conservation law --- against the
fixed prior's convergence).}

% Numerics of Theorem D'''.23 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/adaptive_prior.md).
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_adaptive_prior.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/adaptive_prior.md}). The averaged slow
loop at $\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$ with the OT leak toward a misplaced prior --- the matching prior's
centres shifted by $+0.15$, masses $1/3$. \emph{The linear theory.} At the exact representation the whitened weighted
Gauss--Newton spectrum is $h_j = 3.06\cdot10^{-4}\ \dots\ 0.195$. A fixed prior keeps the fraction
$\sigma_0/(h_j + \sigma_0)$ of its error, 0.959 in the weakest mode and 0.035 in the strongest, with collective rates
$h_j + \sigma_0\ge7.38\cdot10^{-3}$; the adaptive prior's slowest rate is $\lambda_1^- = 2.68\cdot10^{-5}$,
$1.51\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.78\cdot10^{-4}$, $3.06\cdot10^{-4}$ for $\eta = \sigma_0/10$, $\sigma_0$, $10\sigma_0$,
$\infty$. \emph{(v)'s radius.} A sampled lower bound on the Jacobian-Lipschitz constant of the collective block is
$C_N\ge0.341$, so the proof's radius is at most $2.0\cdot10^{-5}$, $1.1\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.0\cdot10^{-4}$,
$2.2\cdot10^{-4}$ for the four $\eta$ --- $\lambda_0/\sigma_0 = 0.0038$--$0.043$ times Theorem D$'''$.18's at the same
$C_N$ --- while the $K_0$-atom runs below start at $\tnorm{\cdot}_\eta = 1.17$, $0.37$, $0.12$, $0.021$, 94 to
$6\cdot10^4$ times outside, the warm clouds of the $m$-particle systems $2.2\cdot10^4$--$3.6\cdot10^5$ times and the
separated starts of Part F 131--3344 times: they probe the dynamics beyond the proved ball. \emph{The fixed prior's
bias.} $\Theta^*(q)$ has the fit error $9.78\cdot10^{-3}$, and the first-order formula
$y^* = \sigma_0(H_L + \sigma_0I)^{-1}x^0$ describes it to 4.8\% in norm; per mode the weak modes 1 and 3 hold 0.85 and
0.73 of the prediction, and the error is first order in the shift (halving the shift from 0.15 to 0.01875 takes the
norm error through 4.8, 2.3, 1.2, 0.58\% and modes 1 and 3 to 0.98 and 0.97 of the prediction). The weakest mode keeps
0.814 of its error, against the predicted 0.959. \emph{The $K_0$-atom loop} from $\Theta^*(q)$, with the prior at the
shifted centres, for the four $\eta$ over 6000 slow units: every mode amplitude above $10^{-6}$ --- thirteen of the 36
--- decays at its predicted rate (log-slopes on $[3000,6000]$ between 0.981 and 1.003 times $\lambda_j^-$), at a ratio
to the exact pair solution between 0.82 and 1.12 that changes by at most 1.04\% from $s = 1000$ on for
$\eta\ge\sigma_0$ (6.0\% at $\sigma_0/10$; the offset is set in the first, nonlinear phase); below $10^{-6}$ the
faster modes stop at the level of the second-order remainder. The fit error falls from $9.78\cdot10^{-3}$ to
$3.6\cdot10^{-4}$, $1.8\cdot10^{-5}$, $8.0\cdot10^{-6}$, $6.7\cdot10^{-6}$ at $s = 6000$, while the weakest mode still
holds $1.45\cdot10^{-3}$, $6.7\cdot10^{-4}$, $3.0\cdot10^{-4}$, $2.5\cdot10^{-4}$ of its $1.58\cdot10^{-3}$: the fit is
restored long before the parameters, as (iii) says. \emph{$m$-particle systems:} $m = 15$, $60$, $240$ and $M = 510$
from the warm clouds of Proposition D$'''$.12's numerics, four seeds, 3000 slow units. Under the fixed prior every
system ends at the lift of $\Theta^*(q)$ ($\norm{\bar y} = 2.10\cdot10^{-2}$,
$W^2(\mu_\Theta,\mu_{\mathrm{exact}}) = 4.4\cdot10^{-4}$, fit error $9.78\cdot10^{-3}$). Ungated, the adaptive prior is
dragged by the transit: at $s = 500$ $\norm{\bar y}$ is 0.032--0.32 ($\eta = \sigma_0$) and 0.048--0.71
($\eta = \infty$) in seed means, against the $K_0$-atom run's $7.4\cdot10^{-3}$ and $4.3\cdot10^{-3}$, and at
$s = 3000$ still 0.0094--0.10 and 0.012--0.59, the largest at $m = 15$. At $\eta = \infty$ one $m = 15$ system is
carried onto a plateau of the unregularised loss: its split class re-collapses by $s = 7000$, onto atoms where the
loss's Hessian has the eigenvalues $-1.8\cdot10^{-5}$ and $7.2\cdot10^{-5}$ (fit error $3.46\cdot10^{-2}$ at
$s = 23000$, the drift $1.4\cdot10^{-5}$). The collapsed $\eta = \infty$ dynamics from there, the unregularised
$K_0$-atom gradient flow, falls below 0.9 of the plateau's loss $2.52\cdot10^{-4}$ after $4.16\cdot10^4$ more slow
units, halves it at $4.78\cdot10^4$ and falls below $10^{-12}$ at $7.97\cdot10^4$ (the crossings located as events),
at the exact representation: the plateau is crossed on the time scale $1/1.84\cdot10^{-5} = 5.4\cdot10^4$ of its
negative curvature. The transit gate opens at $s = 88$--$149$ and removes the plateau, but the transient's residue in
the weak modes stays: $\norm{\bar y} = 4.9\cdot10^{-3}$--$3.7\cdot10^{-2}$ ($\eta = \sigma_0$) at $s = 3000$, against
the $K_0$-atom run's $1.3\cdot10^{-3}$, decreasing with $m$. The settled gate opens at $s = 1211$--$1304$, with the
systems replicated (rms separations $1.2\cdot10^{-4}$--$7.9\cdot10^{-4}$) and their class means within
$6.7\cdot10^{-5}$ of $\Theta^*(q)$; each is then the $K_0$-atom system started from its own class means, and at
$s = 3000$ $\norm{\bar y}$ and the fit error of every system equal the $K_0$-atom run's at $s - t_{\mathrm{open}}$ ---
per-seed ratios 0.995--1.000 ($\eta = \sigma_0$) and 0.968--1.001 ($\eta = \infty$) for every $m$ from 15 to 510, as
the invariance of the replicated configurations requires, the departures from 1 being the opening offset in the
weakest mode --- with fit errors $1.6\cdot10^{-4}$ ($\eta = \sigma_0$) and $3.4$--$3.5\cdot10^{-5}$ ($\eta = \infty$)
against the fixed prior's $9.78\cdot10^{-3}$, $W^2(\mu_\Theta,\mu_{\mathrm{exact}}) = 6.7$--$7.2\cdot10^{-6}$ and
$9.3$--$9.9\cdot10^{-7}$ against $4.4\cdot10^{-4}$, and the counts at $q$ in every run. The wait costs 1211--1304 of
the 3000 slow units: in the seed means the settled gate's $\norm{\bar y}$ and $W^2$ are the lower everywhere, and its
fit error at $\eta = \infty$ and at $m\le240$ (at $\eta = \sigma_0$ and $m = 510$ the transit gate's fit error is the
lower, $1.55\cdot10^{-4}$ against $1.64\cdot10^{-4}$); system by system the transit gate ends lower in all three in 6
of the 32 systems. \emph{The structure behind (v), beyond its ball} (Part F): from the lift of $\Theta^*(q)$ plus
zero-mean per-particle separations of rms 0.02 and 0.1 ($m = 15$ and $510$, ungated, five draws of their direction),
$\norm{\bar y}$ at $s = 1750$ departs from the $K_0$-atom run's, in the draw means, by $+0.13$\% and $+3.1$\% at
$\eta = \sigma_0$ and by $+0.77$\% and $+18.5$\% at $\eta = \infty$ for $m = 15$ ($+0.09$\%, $+2.2$\%, $+0.63$\% and
$+15.5$\% for $m = 510$): second order in the separations draw by draw (factors 23.8--25.0 for the factor 5), with a
spread across the draws 3.3--6.0 times larger at $m = 15$, and consistent with the same expected size at both $m$ (the
draw means, higher at $m = 15$ in all four columns, differ by $+0.9$ to $+1.7$ combined standard errors; five draws)
--- the prediction of Theorem D$'''$.18's facts (1)--(2) at second order, the departure being linear in the
separations' class covariances, whose expectation is the same at every $m$. The starts lie 131--3344 times outside
(v)'s ball, so (v) itself asserts nothing there. \emph{The limits of (i)} (Part G). On a four-bump target that six
kernels fit exactly with one class split ($K_0 = 3$, $m = 6$), the joint flow has a stationary saddle with the class
split --- the pair at $\Ginv$-distance 1.64, against the exact fit's 3.29 --- held by a leak force $5.8\cdot10^{-3}$
against an unregularised gradient of the same size (fit error $4.2\cdot10^{-2}$); from the exact fit the flow
collapses the class within 8000 slow units (at $s = 4\cdot10^4$, $\eta = \infty$: the merged width 1.05, fit error
$2.23\cdot10^{-2}$), and the merged atom then widens toward $s_{\max}$ --- continued, its width coordinate runs off
like $\ln s$ and the width tends to $s_{\max}$ like $1/s$, as in (b): the solution is unbounded --- while its physical
image approaches the minimiser of the loss on the face $s = s_{\max}$ (fit error $1.943\cdot10^{-2}$, a
Karush--Kuhn--Tucker point with $\partial L/\partial s = -1.3\cdot10^{-6}$, not a critical point), reached only as
$s\to\infty$: the $K_0$-cluster bias drives the solution from an exact fit to infinity while $\mathcal{J}$ decreases.
With a target bump of weighted amplitude $4.8 > a_{\max} = 4$, the fixed prior's $K_0$-atom solution converges (fit
error 0.175), while under the adaptive prior ($\eta = \sigma_0$ and $\infty$) the amplitude coordinate grows like
$2\ln s$ (its increments per decade $1.99\ln10$ by $s = 10^6$) and the amplitude tends to $a_{\max}$ like $1/s$: the
solution leaves every compact set. At the exact representation (equal masses) a cyclically permuted prior is
stationary (drift $1.1\cdot10^{-17}$), with $z_{\sigma(k)} = \vartheta_k$.

% Proposition D'''.24 with its proof, Status, numerics and planar check, and Remark D'''.25 with its design table
% (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-19 version; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/adaptive_floor.md and
% results/chaos/adaptive_floor_2d.md).
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.24}[the finite-size floor is the leak's bias at the sampled weights; an adaptive prior
removes it without a mass constraint]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:24}%
Let the target admit, for the weights $w$ in a neighbourhood $W$ of $q$, an exact weighted $K_0$-atom representation
$\vartheta^e(w)$ --- $f = \sum_kw_k\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^e_k(w))$, a critical point of the unregularised
weighted loss whose whitened weighted Gauss--Newton block $H_L(w)$ is invertible, smooth in $w$ --- and let the prior
be matching, $\vartheta^0 = \vartheta^e(q)$. (On the instance the network's amplitude--mass degeneracy gives
$\vartheta^e(w)$ explicitly: the amplitudes $A_k/w_k$ with the bumps' centres and widths, on the branch of
$\vartheta^e(q)$ while $\abs{A_k}/w_k < a_{\max}$; these weights are the \emph{representable} ones, $W$. They are not
Proposition D$'''$.5's admissible set $W_{\mathrm{adm}}$ of the fixed prior, where its replicated stationary point is a
strict local minimiser; a sample in $W_{\mathrm{adm}}$ is called \emph{D$'''$.5-admissible}.)
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the floor is the bias)} Under the nearest-atom leak toward the fixed prior, the weighted stationary
    point $\Theta^*(w)$ of Proposition D$'''$.5 satisfies, with Theorem D$'''$.23's whitened weighted coordinates at
    the weights $w$,
    \begin{gather*}
      \fhat_{\Theta^*(w)} - f = \hat J(w)\sigma_0(H_L(w) + \sigma_0I)^{-1}x^0(w) + O(\norm{x^0(w)}^2),\\
      x^0_k(w) := \sqrt{w_k}G^{-1/2}(\vartheta^0_k - \vartheta^e_k(w)),
    \end{gather*}
    $\hat J(w) := [\sqrt{w_k}\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta^e_k(w))G^{1/2}]_k$ (so that
    $\hat J^\T\hat J = H_L$): the leak's bias at the sampled weights, zero at $w = q$. The function-level
    weight-sensitivity of Propositions D$'''$.5 and D$'''$.9 is its derivative,
    $F = \hat J\sigma_0(H_L + \sigma_0I)^{-1}\partial_wx^0$ at $q$ --- exactly, the remainder's derivative vanishing
    with $x^0(q)$ --- and on the eigenvectors $v_j$ of $H_L(q)$ (eigenvalues $h_j$) the function-level floor law of
    Proposition D$'''$.9 reads
    \begin{equation}
      \E\norm{\fhat_m - \fhat_M}^2 = (1/m - 1/M)\cdot\sum_j\frac{h_j\sigma_0^2}{(h_j + \sigma_0)^2}\cdot
      z_j^\T\tilde Cz_j + o(1/m),\qquad z := V^\T\partial_wx^0,
      \tag{B}
    \end{equation}
    $\partial_w$ taken along the zero-sum directions $e_j - e_{K_0}$ and $\tilde C$ the multinomial covariance of
    $(w_1,\dots,w_{K_0-1})$ --- Proposition D$'''$.9's law written on the eigenvectors, and like it a law for samples
    in $W_{\mathrm{adm}}$: at a saddle the replicated stationary point is left by a split (Proposition D$'''$.5), and
    the floor is the split configuration's. Each mode is weighted by
    $h_j\sigma_0^2/(h_j + \sigma_0)^2\le\sigma_0/4$, with equality at $h_j = \sigma_0$: a band-pass centred at the
    level of the leak, at least half its peak for $h_j/\sigma_0\in[3 - 2\sqrt2,3 + 2\sqrt2]$ (a factor 5.8 either
    side). The law is therefore at most $(1/m - 1/M)(\sigma_0/4)\tr(\partial_wx^0\tilde C\partial_wx^{0\T})$, whatever
    the excitation spectrum. A weakly excited mode keeps its bias but hardly shows it in the function; a stiff one
    shows little bias; which modes carry the floor is set first by the gauge's displacement $\partial_wx^0$ and then
    re-weighted toward $h\approx\sigma_0$.
  \item \emph{(the nearest-atom leak toward an adaptive prior: the masses minimised out)} Let the prior's atoms follow
    the means of their Voronoi cells, $\dd z_j/\dd s = -\eta(z_j - \hat T_j)$ ($\eta > 0$) with $\hat T_j$ the mean
    of the particles nearest to $z_j$, while the particles follow the nearest-atom leak toward $Z$. Inside the cells
    this is the gradient flow of
    \[
      \mathcal{J}_\circ(\Theta,Z) := \bar L(\mu_\Theta)
      + (\sigma_0/2m)\sum_i\min_j\norm{\vartheta_i - z_j}^2_{\Ginv}
      = \min_{q'\in\Delta}\mathcal{J}(\mu_\Theta,\sum_jq'_j\delta_{z_j})
    \]
    in the metric $(1/m)\Ginv$ per particle and $(\sigma_0w_j/\eta)\Ginv$ per atom, $w$ the cell masses: the OT leak
    of Theorem D$'''$.23 with the prior's masses minimised out (semi-relaxed transport --- the infinitely fast limit of
    a dynamics of the masses, not a learning law for them), which sends every particle to its nearest atom. The counts
    change only when a particle crosses into another cell; across a crossing the flow is the vertex-selection flow of
    $\mathcal{J}_\circ$ --- a minimum over the assignments of smooth functions, in a metric that depends on the active
    assignment --- and $\mathcal{J}_\circ$ stays non-increasing, as in Theorem D$'''$.23(i). A cell without particles
    has a degenerate metric: its atom is frozen, and the cell is refilled only if a particle's own dynamics carries it
    in (on the instance an empty cell stays empty; tests).
  \item \emph{(no function-level floor at the representable samples)} At fixed counts $N$, $w = N/m$, the stationary
    replicated configurations are the critical points of the unregularised weighted $K_0$-atom loss at $w$ (Theorem
    D$'''$.23(i) with $w$ in place of $q$), and for $w\in W$ the exact representation $\vartheta^e(w)$ with the prior
    at its atoms is one, with $\fhat = f$. At representable sampled weights the function-level sensitivity therefore
