$\ip{\vartheta_i - \Pi\vartheta_i}{\vartheta_i - z}_{\Ginv}\ge d^2$, so
$(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}s)\frac12d^2\le Bd - \sigma_0d^2$, i.e.\ $d'\le B - \sigma_0d$ where $d > 0$, and the
comparison principle gives the bound; boundedness follows from that of $\mathcal{Z}$. The bound on $B$:
$\norm{G\langle r\nabla\tilde\Phi\rangle}_{\Ginv} = \norm{\langle rG^{1/2}\nabla\tilde\Phi\rangle}
\le\sup\abs{r}\cdot L_\vartheta^G$ and $\abs{r}\le\abs{f} + \abs{\fhat}$. (ii) The particles' part of
$\dd\mathcal{J}/\dd s$ is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(i)'s, minus the squared norm of their
velocity; the prior's is
$\ip{\nabla_Z\mathcal{J}}{\Pi_T(-c\nabla_Z\mathcal{J})} = -\norm{\Pi_T(-c\nabla_Z\mathcal{J})}^2/c$ ($c$ the metric's
factor; Moreau's decomposition of $-c\nabla_Z\mathcal{J}$ into its tangent-cone and normal-cone parts, which are
orthogonal), so $\mathcal{J}$ is non-increasing. At $\eta = \infty$ the prior minimises the $W^2$ part over
$\mathcal{Z}$ for the current plan: for each plan $\gamma$,
$\min_Z\mathcal{J} = \bar L + (\sigma_0/2)\sum_j\bigl[\sum_i\gamma_{ij}\norm{\vartheta_i - \hat T_j}^2_{\Ginv}
+ q_j\operatorname{dist}^2_{\Ginv}(\hat T_j,\mathcal{Z})\bigr]$ (minimising
$\sum_i\gamma_{ij}\norm{\vartheta_i - z}^2 = q_j\norm{z - \hat T_j}^2 + \mathrm{const}$ over $z\in\mathcal{Z}$), each
piece $C^{1,1}$ and definable (the squared distance to a box is piecewise quadratic), and its gradient in
$\vartheta_i$ is the learning term and the leak toward $\Pi_{\mathcal{Z}}\hat T_j$ (the derivative of
$q_j\operatorname{dist}^2(\hat T_j,\mathcal{Z})$ adds $\gamma_{ij}(\hat T_j - \Pi_{\mathcal{Z}}\hat T_j)$ to the
within-class term). Between plan refreshes the particles follow the gradient flow of the piece
$E_\gamma(\Theta) := \min_Z\mathcal{J}(\Theta,Z;\gamma)$ of the plan $\gamma$ they carry, and at a refresh the new
plan is optimal for the current prior and the prior then re-minimises, so the selected energy does not increase there
either. But $\gamma$ is Lloyd-consistent --- optimal for $\nu_Z$ with $Z = \Pi_{\mathcal{Z}}\hat T(\gamma)$ --- and
need not attain $\min_\gamma E_\gamma = \min_Z\mathcal{J}$: the flow is then not a subgradient trajectory of the
reduced energy, and Proposition D$'''$.17's argument, whose fixed prior makes every selected piece the active one,
does not apply as it stands (Part H(e): the reduced energy rises by 26\% while the selected energy falls). A
Kurdyka--{\L}ojasiewicz argument on the finitely many definable pieces that share the limiting value should give the
bounded solutions finite length, with a critical point of a Lloyd-consistent piece as the limit; it is not carried out
here. At finite $\eta$ the joint energy carries the indicator of $\mathcal{Z}^{K_0}$, and the Kurdyka--{\L}ojasiewicz
argument with it is not carried out here. At a critical point the projected velocity of every atom vanishes, which is
the stated condition. (iii) The projection is inactive in $\operatorname{int}\mathcal{Z}$, and
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(v)'s ball keeps the prior within $\rho(\eta/(\sigma_0q_k))^{1/2}$ of the
atoms. (iv) If no stationary point of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(i) has its prior in
$\operatorname{int}\mathcal{Z}$, a critical point of (ii) has an atom on $\partial\mathcal{Z}$. At $\eta = \infty$
with a class collapsed at $\vartheta$, $\hat T = \vartheta$ and $z = \Pi_{\mathcal{Z}}\vartheta$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved; (ii) the monotonicity of the selected energy and the characterisation of the critical points
proved, the convergence argued at $\eta = \infty$ (a Kurdyka--{\L}ojasiewicz argument on the selected pieces, not
carried out) and formal at finite $\eta$; (iii) proved, its local convergence as
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(v) (sketched); (iv) the identification at $\eta = \infty$ on collapsed
classes proved, the boundary atoms and the bias outside numerical. The bound of (i) is crude (it uses the worst-case
force; $L_\vartheta^G\le4.658$ is the closed-form bound, 4.044 by search), and its content is qualitative:
boundedness is no longer a hypothesis. Parameter projection onto a convex set, the exterior quadratic penalty and the
switching $\sigma$-modification are classical robustness modifications of adaptive laws, and the switching
$\sigma$-modification keeps the ideal (unbiased) properties of the adaptive law when the true parameter lies in its
set (\cite{ioannou1996}, \cite{S52}); on collapsed classes at $\eta = \infty$ the construction is that law. New is its
transfer to the learned centre of a mass-constrained $K_0$-atom prior at finite $\eta$ --- a projected adaptive law
for the prior's atoms, whose velocity jumps at the faces while the particles' law keeps its smooth gradient form ---
and its compatibility with \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}'s bias removal wherever the target is
representable inside $\mathcal{Z}$. Checked: \code{tests/test_adaptive_prior.py} (beyond the saturation, the
projected prior at $\eta = \infty$ (LSODA) keeps the particles within 2 of the box and converges, while without the
box the prior's coordinate passes 11 by $s = 10^4$; at $\eta = \sigma_0$ the projected midpoint rule at
$\mathrm{d}t = 0.125$ keeps the prior in the box, brings its amplitude atom onto the face, keeps the particles bounded
and $\mathcal{J}$ non-increasing; at the misplaced prior near the exact representation the projected and unprojected
runs are identical).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.27 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/adaptive_prior.md, Part H).
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_adaptive_prior.py} Part H $\to$ \code{results/chaos/adaptive_prior.md}). The box
$\mathcal{Z}$ holds the amplitudes and centres to 0.9 of $a_{\max}$ and $c_{\max}$ ($\alpha\in[-5.889,5.889]$,
$\gamma\in[-4.417,4.417]$) and the width coordinate in $[-6,6]$; the instance's exact atoms are inside. (a) Part
G(b)'s saturated target (a weighted amplitude $4.8 > a_{\max}$), the $K_0$-atom system from the exact atoms with the
prior there: under the projected prior both $\eta = \sigma_0$ (the projected midpoint rule --- positions projected
inside the step --- at $\mathrm{d}t = 0.25$ and $0.125$, to $s = 10^5$) and $\eta = \infty$ (LSODA, rtol $10^{-10}$)
converge to the same bounded stationary point (largest drift $1.8\cdot10^{-15}$, $3.5\cdot10^{-15}$ and
$1.2\cdot10^{-16}$; the two steps' end states within $3.4\cdot10^{-12}$), the first prior atom's amplitude coordinate
on the face of $\mathcal{Z}$ ($z_a = 5.889$) and the particle's at 6.728 (amplitude 3.7325), with the fit error
0.1415 --- the two steps' agreement shows their shared fixed point; the scheme's consistency: at $s = 300$ the
trajectory lies within $6.3\cdot10^{-7}$, $1.9\cdot10^{-7}$ and $3.8\cdot10^{-8}$ of a reference at
$\mathrm{d}t = 1/64$ for $\mathrm{d}t = 0.25$, $0.125$, $0.0625$. The particles' largest $\Ginv$-distance from
$\mathcal{Z}$ along the run is 0.92 ($\eta = \sigma_0$) and 0.89 ($\eta = \infty$), against the bound
$B/\sigma_0\le3686$ ($B\le26.08$ with the closed-form $L_\vartheta^G$). (b) Part C's $K_0$-atom loop at the misplaced
prior ($\eta = \sigma_0$, 6000 slow units) is identical with and without $\mathcal{Z}$ (largest difference 0.0): the
box is never approached --- the prior and the particles keep at least 16.9\% of the box's width to every face --- so
the two runs coincide by construction. (c) The box trades the fit error against the parameters' bound: with the
amplitude bound 0.75, 0.8, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99 of $a_{\max}$ --- boxes that contain the instance's exact atoms --- the
limit's fit error is 0.1676, 0.1611, 0.1415, 0.1268, 0.1101, decreasing between the fixed prior's 0.1750 and the
unprojected adaptive prior's 0.1050 at $s = 10^6$ (which that prior approaches only as its amplitude coordinate runs
off), the prior's amplitude atom on the face in every case; the bound 0.7 (0.1736) already excludes the exact atoms
(the start is projected at once), and the tighter boxes 0.65, 0.6, 0.5 are worse than the fixed prior (0.1792,
0.1845, 0.1949): a box should contain the representation. (e) At $\eta = \infty$ on six particles in a
Lloyd-consistent, non-optimal pairing (a seeded search; zero amplitudes, a small three-bump target, the box) the
selected energy falls from $4.52\cdot10^{-3}$ to $4.95\cdot10^{-5}$ (its largest increment $-3.4\cdot10^{-6}$) while
the reduced energy $\min_Z\mathcal{J}$ rises from $1.251\cdot10^{-3}$ to $1.574\cdot10^{-3}$ before the plan becomes
optimal at $s = 80$. (d) On Part G(a)'s four-bump target, where without the box the merged atom's width coordinate
grows like $\ln s$ and the solution leaves every compact set, the box stops it: from the exact split fit at
$\eta = \infty$ (the joint flow for 40000 slow units, then the collapsed dynamics) $\kappa$ passes 2.159, 3.843, 5.529
at $s = 10^6$, $10^7$, $10^8$ as before and stays at the face $\kappa = 6$ from $s = 10^9$ (the atom within
$6.6\cdot10^{-7}$ of it, drift $5.3\cdot10^{-17}$), with the fit error $1.943376\cdot10^{-2}$, within
$9.3\cdot10^{-7}$ of the minimiser's on the face $s = s_{\max}$ that the unprojected solution approaches only as
$s\to\infty$.

% Proposition D'''.28 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-20 version; the numbers are
% PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/gauge_floor.md). The references [49], [55] (PARADIGM.md) and [S55]
% (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) are the bibitems R49, R55 and S55 of refs.tex.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.28}[the adaptive prior's floor removal is a gauge property: it survives an
approximation error]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:28}%
Let $\vartheta^b(q)$ be a nondegenerate critical point of the unregularised weighted loss
$L_q(\Theta) := \frac12\langle\abs{f - \sum_kq_k\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_k)}^2\rangle_\rho$ --- not necessarily an
exact representation: $r_b := f - \fhat_b$ may be non-zero --- and let the masses enter the network only through the
products $w_ka(\alpha_k)$, as they do for the saturated network. The amplitude--mass gauge
$G_w : \alpha_k\mapsto a^{-1}(q_ka(\alpha_k)/w_k)$, the other coordinates unchanged, is defined on
$W_g := \{w : q_k\abs{a^b_k} < w_ka_{\max}\text{ for every }k\}$ and satisfies $\fhat_w\circ G_w = \fhat_q$, hence
$L_w\circ G_w = L_q$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the stationary points carry no function-level floor)} For every $w\in W_g$,
    $\vartheta^b(w) := G_w\vartheta^b(q)$ is a nondegenerate critical point of $L_w$ with the same function
    $\fhat_b$, the same residual $r_b$ and the same Hessian signature. With the prior at its atoms it is a stationary
    replicated configuration of the nearest-atom leak toward an adaptive prior
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(iii)), so two systems at their samples' gauge transforms carry
    the same function whatever the approximation error, with the fit error $\norm{r_b}_\rho$. The function-level floor
    --- the limit of $\E\norm{\fhat_m - \fhat_M}^2$ along the dynamics --- therefore vanishes at the samples of $W_g$
    with positive margins in (iii) when $\vartheta^b(q)$ is a strict local minimum and the systems converge to the
    gauge transforms (numerical); $W_g$ shrinks as the approximation error grows (its thresholds
    $q_k\abs{a^b_k}/a_{\max}$ rise with the amplitudes). (At an exact representation $W_g$ is
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}'s $W$.)
  \item \emph{(the fixed prior's floor)} At the matching prior $\vartheta^0 = \vartheta^b(q)$ the function-level
    weight-sensitivity of the fixed prior's stationary point is
    \[
      F = \sigma_0\hat J(\tilde A + \sigma_0I)^{-1}\tilde g,\qquad\tilde A := H_L - \tilde R,
    \]
    $\tilde A$ the whitened weighted Hessian of $L_q$ at $\vartheta^b(q)$ ($\tilde R$ the whitened weighted residual
    curvature) and $\tilde g$ the whitened gauge displacement, the direct effect of the weights on $\fhat$ being
    $\hat J\tilde g$: \hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(i) with the full Hessian in place of the
    Gauss--Newton block. Along the same derivation the adaptive prior's sensitivity is $F(\sigma)$ at $\sigma = 0$,
    which vanishes, as (i) says.
  \item \emph{(the splitting margins under the adaptive prior)} For a scalar amplitude per kernel (one output), at a
    critical point the residual curvature
    $R_k = \langle r\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_k)\rangle_\rho$ has a zero amplitude row and
    column, and its block in the centre and width coordinates is $a(\alpha_k)$ times a function of those coordinates
    alone. Hence $R_k(\vartheta^b(w)) = (q_k/w_k)R_k(\vartheta^b(q))$ exactly, and Proposition D$'''$.5's splitting
    margins at the adaptive prior's replicated stationary point are
    \[
      \sigma_0 - (q_k/w_k)\lambda^+_k,\qquad
      \lambda^+_k := \lambda_{\max}(G^{1/2}R_k(\vartheta^b(q))G^{1/2})\ge0:
    \]
    a class sampled below $q_k\lambda^+_k/\sigma_0$ can split. At an exact representation $\lambda^+ = 0$ and every
    margin is $\sigma_0$ (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(iii)); with an approximation error, and
    $\vartheta^b(q)$ a strict local minimum, the adaptive prior's admissible set is
    $\{w\in W_g : w_k > q_k\lambda^+_k/\sigma_0\}$. For vector amplitudes ($n\ge2$ outputs, $a(\alpha)\in\R^n$
    saturated componentwise) only the centre--width block scales by $q_k/w_k$: stationarity in the amplitudes gives
    $\Psi_r = 0$ for every output $r$ but stationarity in the centres only $\sum_ra_r\partial_\gamma\Psi_r = 0$, so
    the amplitude--centre block $a'_r(\alpha_r)\partial_\gamma\Psi_r$ survives, is gauge-invariant in the physical
    amplitudes, and no closed margin formula follows.
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) $G_w$ is a diffeomorphism from $\{\alpha : \abs{q_ka(\alpha)/w_k} < a_{\max}\}$ onto its image with
$\fhat_w(G_w\Theta) = \fhat_q(\Theta)$, so $L_w\circ G_w = L_q$, and it maps critical points to critical points with
the same Hessian signature; the function and the residual are unchanged by construction. The leak toward an adaptive
prior at its atoms vanishes (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(i),
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(ii)). (ii) Let $u := \partial_w(G_w\vartheta^b(q))$ at $w = q$,
$D := \operatorname{diag}(q_k\Ginv)$, $A := \nabla^2L_q(\vartheta^b)$, $B := \partial_w\nabla_\Theta L_w$ at fixed
$\Theta$, $J := \partial_\Theta\fhat$, $\Phi := \partial_w\fhat$ at fixed $\Theta$. Differentiating
$\fhat_w(G_w\Theta) = \fhat_q(\Theta)$ gives $\Phi = -Ju$, and differentiating $\nabla L_w(G_w\vartheta^b) = 0$ gives
$B = -Au$. The fixed prior's stationarity $\nabla L_w(\Theta) + \sigma_0D(\Theta - \vartheta^0) = 0$, linearised at
$(q,\vartheta^b)$, reads $(A + \sigma_0D)\delta\Theta = -B\delta w = Au\delta w$, so
$\delta\fhat = \Phi\delta w + J\delta\Theta = -J(A + \sigma_0D)^{-1}[(A + \sigma_0D) - A]u\delta w
= -\sigma_0J(A + \sigma_0D)^{-1}Du\delta w$; in the whitened coordinates ($\hat J = JD^{-1/2}$,
$\tilde A = D^{-1/2}AD^{-1/2}$, $\tilde g := -D^{1/2}u$) this is $\sigma_0\hat J(\tilde A + \sigma_0I)^{-1}\tilde g$.
With $\sigma_0 = 0$, $\delta\Theta = u\delta w$ and $\delta\fhat = 0$. At an exact representation $\tilde R = 0$,
$\tilde A = H_L$ and $\tilde g = \partial_wx^0$. (iii) With a scalar amplitude and
$\Psi(\gamma,\kappa) := \langle r\varphi(\cdot\,;c(\gamma),s(\kappa))\rangle_\rho$,
$\langle r\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta)\rangle_\rho = a(\alpha)\Psi$, whose gradient is
$(a'\Psi,a\Psi_\gamma,a\Psi_\kappa)$ and whose Hessian is
$\left[\begin{smallmatrix}a''\Psi & a'\Psi_\gamma & a'\Psi_\kappa\\ a'\Psi_\gamma & a\Psi_{\gamma\gamma}
& a\Psi_{\gamma\kappa}\\ a'\Psi_\kappa & a\Psi_{\gamma\kappa} & a\Psi_{\kappa\kappa}\end{smallmatrix}\right]$. At a
critical point the gradient vanishes, so $\Psi = \Psi_\gamma = \Psi_\kappa = 0$ ($a' > 0$, $a\ne0$) and only the
block $a\Psi_{(\gamma,\kappa)}$ remains; $r$ and $\Psi$ are unchanged by the gauge and $a$ scales by $q_k/w_k$. The
$\alpha$-direction contributes the eigenvalue 0, so $\lambda^+_k\ge0$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) and (iii) proved, exactly, as statements about the stationary points and their linearisation (no
linearisation in $w$: the gauge maps critical points to critical points); (ii) as
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(i) --- exact at $q$, first order in $w$; the convergence of the
dynamics to the gauge transform numerical, and with it the floor's vanishing. The amplitude--mass degeneracy is the
homogeneity of the conic view \cite{R49}, \cite{R55}, and the rescaling symmetries of networks, which map critical
points to critical points with the same function while changing their curvature, are classical (\cite{S55}); what is
new is that this symmetry, not exact representability, is the mechanism of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}'s floor removal --- so at a strict local minimum in the interior the
adaptive prior keeps the fitted function independent of the sampled weights without an exact representation --- and,
for a scalar amplitude per kernel, the exact scaling of the residual curvature under the gauge, which gives the
adaptive prior's splitting margins in closed form. Its hypotheses are a strict local minimum in the interior and
samples in $W_g$ with positive margins: on the instance $P(w^m\in W_g)$ is only 0.52 at $m = 15$ (0.87 at $m = 60$),
and the branch ends at the saturation near $\varepsilon = 0.68$; targets whose best approximant lies on the saturation
boundary (a sharp friction step fitted by a dipole of two kernels; the cogging servo of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:29]{Proposition D$'''$.29}, globally; \hyperref[res:thmDppp:27]{Proposition D$'''$.27}'s
beyond-the-saturation example) are outside it. Checked: \code{tests/test_gauge_floor.py} (at $\varepsilon = 0.3$, a
critical point with a 1.7\% fit error: the gauge transform a critical point at two sampled weight vectors, the
critical point continued in $w$ from $\vartheta^b(q)$ landing on it; the adaptive prior's sensitivity, from critical
points reached by Newton from $\vartheta^b(q)$, below $10^{-8}$ of the fixed prior's, the fixed prior's equal to
$\sigma_0\hat J(\tilde A + \sigma_0I)^{-1}\tilde g$ to $10^{-6}$ and not to the Gauss--Newton form; the residual
curvature's zero amplitude row, its scaling $(q_k/w_k)$, the margins $\sigma_0 - (q_k/w_k)\lambda^+_k$ and the
dynamics' separation eigenvalues $-\sigma_0 + (q_k/w_k)\operatorname{eig}(G^{1/2}R_kG^{1/2})$ to $10^{-9}$;
adaptive-prior runs at $\eta = \infty$ and $\sigma_0$ converging to the gauge transform, 1.2 away from
$\vartheta^b(q)$, with the approximant's function).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.28 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/gauge_floor.md).
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_gauge_floor.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/gauge_floor.md}). The three-bump instance
with a fourth bump $\varepsilon\cdot\exp(-(x - 0.8)^2/(2\cdot0.3^2))$ that $K_0 = 3$ kernels cannot represent,
$\varepsilon = 0$--$0.5$, the masses $q = 1/3$, $\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$; $\vartheta^b(q)$ the weighted
approximant, a critical point of the unregularised loss continued in $\varepsilon$ from the exact representation
(relative fit error $3.6\cdot10^{-3}$, $6.8\cdot10^{-3}$, $1.2\cdot10^{-2}$, $1.7\cdot10^{-2}$, $2.5\cdot10^{-2}$ at
$\varepsilon = 0.05$, $0.1$, $0.2$, $0.3$, $0.5$; gradient residual $\le1.6\cdot10^{-15}$; nondegenerate, the
smallest eigenvalue of $\tilde A$ $4.6\cdot10^{-5}$--$3.1\cdot10^{-4}$). (i) At six weight vectors 0.01 inside $W_g$
the unregularised gradient at $G_w\vartheta^b(q)$ is $\le1.6\cdot10^{-15}$, and Newton's method continued in $w$ from
$\vartheta^b(q)$ (20 steps) lands on $G_w\vartheta^b(q)$ to $\le1.7\cdot10^{-11}$ with $\fhat$ unchanged to
$\le5.5\cdot10^{-13}$ --- plain Newton from $\vartheta^b(q)$ at $w$ does so in 3--5 of the 6 cases, elsewhere
reaching other critical points of $L_w$; the adaptive prior's function-level sensitivity, from the critical points
reached from $\vartheta^b(q)$, is $1.4\cdot10^{-11}$--$2.7\cdot10^{-10}$ of the fixed prior's. The samples fall in
$W_g$ with the probability 0.52, 0.87--0.88, 0.997 at $m = 15$, $60$, $240$ ($\varepsilon = 0.05$--$0.3$; 0.26, 0.71,
0.98 at $\varepsilon = 0.5$), and the branch continued to $\varepsilon = 0.68$ reaches
$\max_k\abs{a^b_k}/a_{\max} = 0.999$, the smallest eigenvalue of $\tilde A$ $1.2\cdot10^{-8}$ and $W_g$'s threshold
$0.333 = q_2$ for its class: there $w = q$ leaves $W_g$. (ii) The fixed matching prior's sensitivity equals
$\sigma_0\hat J(\tilde A + \sigma_0I)^{-1}\tilde g$ to $\le2.2\cdot10^{-9}$ and misses
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}'s Gauss--Newton form by $7.0\cdot10^{-4}$--$5.6\cdot10^{-3}$,
growing with $\varepsilon$ as $\norm{\tilde R}/\norm{H_L}$ does ($5.3\cdot10^{-4}$--$2.8\cdot10^{-3}$); its floor law
per unit of $1/m - 1/M$ rises from $6.17\cdot10^{-2}$ ($\varepsilon = 0$) to $8.52\cdot10^{-2}$
($\varepsilon = 0.5$). (iii) The residual curvature's amplitude row is $\le7.4\cdot10^{-14}$ (its largest entry
$2.6\cdot10^{-4}$--$2.9\cdot10^{-3}$), $\lambda^+_k\le7.2\cdot10^{-4}$ (at most $0.10\sigma_0$), and over every count
vector of $m = 15$ and $60$ in $W_g$ the adaptive prior's splitting margins equal $\sigma_0 - (q_k/w_k)\lambda^+_k$ to
$\le2.1\cdot10^{-14}$, none negative (the smallest $+6.23\cdot10^{-3}$); the separation eigenvalues of the dynamics at
a replicated configuration (counts $(6,4,5)$, the Jacobian of the averaged drift at $\eta = \sigma_0$ and $\infty$)
equal $-\sigma_0 + (q_k/w_k)\operatorname{eig}(G^{1/2}R_kG^{1/2})$ to $\le5.8\cdot10^{-12}$. \emph{The dynamics}
(Part B: warm clouds of width 0.02 around $\vartheta^b(q)$, $m = 15$, $60$, $240$ against $M = 510$, four seeds, 6000
slow units, $\varepsilon = 0.1$ and $0.3$, the nearest-atom leak). Under the fixed prior $\norm{\fhat_m - \fhat_M}^2$
stays within 8\% of its value at $s = 600$ (the ratio at $s = 6000$ 0.917--1.003; at $\varepsilon = 0.1$:
$3.1\cdot10^{-4}$--$5.9\cdot10^{-3}$, $1.1\cdot10^{-4}$--$1.0\cdot10^{-3}$ and $5.0\cdot10^{-6}$--$8.2\cdot10^{-5}$
at $m = 15$, $60$, $240$) and the fit error above the approximant's ($9.0\cdot10^{-3}$--$1.2\cdot10^{-1}$ against
$6.84\cdot10^{-3}$), the classes splitting at the samples where the fixed prior's point is a saddle (diameters up to
1.6). Under the adaptive priors the classes stay replicated, their means approach the gauge transform
$G_w\vartheta^b(q)$, $\norm{\fhat_m - \fhat_M}^2$ falls (at $m = 60$, $\varepsilon = 0.1$, from
$6.9\cdot10^{-6}$--$1.2\cdot10^{-4}$ at $s = 600$ to $1.0\cdot10^{-9}$--$1.4\cdot10^{-8}$ at $\eta = \sigma_0$ and to
$3.2\cdot10^{-11}$--$3.8\cdot10^{-9}$ at $\eta = \infty$ by $s = 6000$), and the fit error tends to the approximant's:
at the samples at least 0.02 inside $W_g$ it exceeds it at $s = 6000$ by a median $1.2\cdot10^{-4}$,
$1.2\cdot10^{-4}$, $1.6\cdot10^{-5}$ (relative; at most $1.1\cdot10^{-1}$, $1.2\cdot10^{-2}$, $1.3\cdot10^{-3}$) at
$m = 15$, $60$, $240$. At the $m = 15$ sample $(0.533,0.267,0.200)$, outside $W_g$ (margins $-0.0016$ and $-0.0097$
at $\varepsilon = 0.1$ and $0.3$), $\vartheta^b(q)$'s branch has no gauge transform: the adaptive priors' fit errors
at $s = 6000$ ($2.2\cdot10^{-2}$ and $1.6\cdot10^{-2}$ at $\varepsilon = 0.1$, $3.7\cdot10^{-2}$ and
$3.3\cdot10^{-2}$ at $\varepsilon = 0.3$, for $\eta = \sigma_0$ and $\infty$) are still falling toward the minimum of
$L_w$ over the closed amplitude box on the branch, 1.107 and 1.586 times the approximant's fit error (one amplitude at
the bound, its coordinate running off), and a gauge transform with two classes exchanged exists there, which the
warm-cloud dynamics does not reach; at $(0.400,0.200,0.400)$, $6\cdot10^{-4}$ inside $W_g$ at $\varepsilon = 0.3$,
the gauge transform's amplitude lies near the saturation and the approach is slow (2.8--3.1 from it at $s = 6000$),
as at the boundary of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}'s $W$.

% Proposition D'''.29 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-20 version; the numbers are
% PARADIGM's copies of results/servo/servo_cogging.md). The references [1] and [6]--[8] (PARADIGM.md) are the bibitems
% sanner1992 and R6, R7, R8 of refs.tex.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.29}[second-order plants: the sliding-variable
reduction]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:29}%
Let the plant be $\ddot x = f(x,\dot x) + u$, $x_m$ a $T_0$-periodic $C^3$ reference, $e := x - x_m$,
$s := \dot e + \lambda e$ ($\lambda > 0$), and let the controller and the adaptive law be
\[
  u = \ddot x_m - \lambda\dot e - k_s s - \fhat_\Theta(z),\qquad
  \dot\vartheta_i = G\nabla_\vartheta(s\tilde\Phi(z;\vartheta_i)) - (\sigma_0/k_s)(\vartheta_i - T_i),
\]
$z$ the coordinates of $(x,\dot x)$ on which $f$ depends and $T_i$ any of the leak targets of this section (a fixed
prior, the nearest-atom or the OT plan, an adaptive or a projected prior).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the error channel)} $\dot s = -k_s s + r_\Theta(z)$ and $\dot e = -\lambda e + s$, with
    $z = z_m + (e,\dot e)$ the regressor at the plant's state: Theorem J's error channel with $s$ in place of $e$.
  \item \emph{(the averaged slow loop)} As $k_s\to\infty$ at fixed $\lambda$, $s$ and $e$ are slaved ---
    $s = (\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_s)^{-1}[r_\Theta(z_m)] + O(k_s^{-2}) = r_\Theta(z_m)/k_s + O(k_s^{-2})$ and
    $e = (\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + \lambda)^{-1}s$, the periodic solutions of the two filters --- and in the slow time
    $\tau = t/k_s$ the parameters follow Theorem J's averaged slow loop along the regressor's orbit $z_m(t)$,
    \[
      \frac{\dd\vartheta_i}{\dd\tau} = G\langle r_\Theta\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(\cdot\,;\vartheta_i)\rangle_\rho
      - \sigma_0(\vartheta_i - T_i),\quad\text{$\rho$ the occupation measure of $z_m$ over one period,}
    \]
    up to $O(1/k_s)$, the prior's law at finite $\eta$ being $\dd z_j/\dd t = -(\eta/k_s)(z_j - \hat T_j)$ in real
    time. Both forms of the slaved error --- the exact filter $(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_s)^{-1}$ and $r/k_s$ ---
    are accurate to $O(k_s^{-2})$ in $s$, i.e.\ to $O(1/k_s)$ relatively, the parameters' within-period motion and the
    nonlinearity $r(z) - r(z_m)$ entering at the same order; the exact filter keeps the error filter's attenuation of
    the residual's harmonics, which $r/k_s$ drops at the relative order $n^2\omega_0^2/(2k_s^2)$ in the rms of the
    $n$-th harmonic, and is the better prediction where that term dominates (a small residual of high frequency).
  \item \emph{(the transfer)} Every statement of this section about the averaged slow loop holds for the second-order
    plant with this $\rho$ --- Theorems D$'''$, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:21]{D$'''$.21} and
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{D$'''$.23}, Propositions D$'''$.5, D$'''$.9, D$'''$.12, D$'''$.17,
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{D$'''$.24}(i)--(v), \hyperref[res:thmDppp:27]{D$'''$.27} and
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:28]{D$'''$.28} --- and the tracking error of the position is the filtered residual of (ii);
    the real-loop statements (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(vi), Corollaries D$'''$.14 and
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{D$'''$.26}) would need the variational equation of (i).
  \item \emph{(where the kernels live)} When $f$ depends on one coordinate (a cogging torque on the position, a
    friction law on the velocity), kernels on that coordinate see the orbit as an interval, and the instance is this
    section's one-dimensional one with another target. Isotropic Gaussian kernels on the phase plane over a circular
    orbit ($x_m = R\sin\omega_0t$ with $\omega_0 = 1$ in the units where the kernel is isotropic in $(x,\dot x)$) are
    over-parametrised: on the circle $z = R(\sin\theta,\cos\theta)$ a kernel restricts to
    $a\cdot e^{-(R^2 + \abs{c}^2)/(2s^2)}\cdot e^{(R\abs{c}/s^2)\cos(\theta - \theta_c)}$, a von Mises function of
    three effective parameters for four, so in general position the Gauss--Newton block has a zero eigenvalue for
    every kernel (more at degenerate atoms, as for kernels centred on the orbit's centre) --- a direction that the
    fixed prior's leak pins at the rate $\sigma_0$, that an adaptive prior leaves neutral ($\lambda^- = 0$,
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(ii)), and that a projected prior keeps bounded
    (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:27]{Proposition D$'''$.27}). On other orbits the same directions are weakly excited rather
    than unexcited: the classical partial excitation of Gaussian networks along an orbit (\cite{R6,R7,R8}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) $\ddot e = f + u - \ddot x_m = r_\Theta(z) - \lambda\dot e - k_s s$, so
$\dot s = \ddot e + \lambda\dot e = -k_s s + r_\Theta(z)$, and $\dot e = s - \lambda e$ by the definition of $s$. (ii)
The error subsystem $(s,e)$ is linear, exponentially stable (eigenvalues $-k_s$ and $-\lambda$) and driven by
$r_\Theta(z)$, while the parameters move at the rate $O(1/k_s)$ (their velocity is $G\nabla_\vartheta(s\tilde\Phi)$
with $s = O(1/k_s)$). Theorem J's argument --- for the smooth fixed-prior leak near a hyperbolic critical point, using
the error channel's exponential stability, the regressor's smoothness and its Step~3's normal form --- carries over
with the fast pair $(s,e)$ once its error-channel steps are redone for it ($s$ slaved at $k_s$, $e$ filtered at the
fixed $\lambda$: the error channel's multipliers are $e^{-k_sT_0}$ and $e^{-\lambda T_0}$), the separation being
between $\lambda$ and the parameters' rate; for switching targets (plan and cell switches, adaptive and projected
priors) not even the first-order loop has more than Theorem D$'''$.18's locally constant case. At the slaved state
$z = z_m + O(1/k_s)$, and the period average of $G\nabla_\vartheta(s\tilde\Phi(z_m;\vartheta_i))$ with
$s = r_\Theta(z_m)/k_s$ is $(1/k_s)$ times the averaged learning term. For the $n$-th harmonic of $r$,
$(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_s)^{-1}$ has the gain
$(k_s^2 + n^2\omega_0^2)^{-1/2} = (1/k_s)(1 - n^2\omega_0^2/(2k_s^2) + \dots)$. (iii) The statements of this section
use the averaged loop only through $\rho$, the target on its support and the network. (iv)
$\abs{z - c}^2 = R^2 + \abs{c}^2 - 2R\abs{c}\cos(\theta - \theta_c)$ on the circle; the map
$(a,\abs{c},\theta_c,s)\mapsto(a e^{-(R^2+\abs{c}^2)/(2s^2)},R\abs{c}/s^2,\theta_c)$ has a one-dimensional fibre,
along which the kernel's restriction, and hence $\fhat$ on $\rho$'s support, is constant.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) and (iv) exact; (ii) and (iii) formal --- first-order averaging with the fast pair $(s,e)$, Theorem J's
steps carrying over for the smooth fixed-prior leak near a hyperbolic critical point once the error-channel steps are
redone, and switching targets beyond even the first-order theory; (iii) covers the statements about the averaged
loop, not the real-loop ones (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(vi) and Corollaries D$'''$.14 and
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{D$'''$.26} would need the variational equation of (i)). The filtered error is the classical
construction of adaptive control for higher-order plants, used with Gaussian networks by Sanner and Slotine
(\cite{sanner1992}); what is added is the transfer of the transport leaks, their floors and the adaptive and projected
priors to such plants, and the exact fibre of isotropic phase-plane kernels on a circular orbit (the weak excitation
of the same directions on other orbits being the classical partial excitation \cite{R6,R7,R8}). Checked:
\code{tests/test_sliding.py} (the phase-plane loop as the gradient flow of $\bar L$; on a circular orbit one zero
Gauss--Newton eigenvalue per kernel in general position, the next above $10^{-11}$ of the largest; the servo's
parameter displacement over ten periods within 3\% of the averaged loop's at $k_s = 25$ and closer at $k_s = 50$, and
its position error within 1\% of both predictions of (ii) at $k_s = 50$).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.29 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/servo/servo_cogging.md).
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_servo_cogging.py} $\to$ \code{results/servo/servo_cogging.md}). The servo
$\ddot x = -\tau_{\mathrm{cog}}(x) + u$ with the cogging torque
$\tau_{\mathrm{cog}}(x) = 0.4\sin(3x + 0.3) + 0.1\sin7x$, $x_m = \sin t$, $\lambda = 5$; $K_0 = 4$ saturated Gaussian
kernels on the position ($a_{\max} = 4$, $c_{\max} = 1.6$, widths in $[0.05,1]$), masses $1/4$, $\sigma_0 = 0.01$. (A)
$\vartheta^b(q)$, the nondegenerate local minimum of the unregularised weighted loss that BFGS reaches from a fixed
start, has the relative fit error $1.46\cdot10^{-2}$, and \hyperref[res:thmDppp:28]{Proposition D$'''$.28} is its
local analysis: the whitened unregularised Hessian $\tilde A$ spans $2.9\cdot10^{-4}\sigma_0$--$30\sigma_0$ (four
modes below $0.1\sigma_0$: the adaptive prior's slowest rates $1.4\cdot10^{-4}\sigma_0$ at $\eta = \sigma_0$ and
$2.9\cdot10^{-4}\sigma_0$ at $\eta = \infty$, against the fixed prior's $\approx\sigma_0$), the residual curvature's
$\lambda^+_k\le0.0098\sigma_0$ (the adaptive prior's margins positive on all of
$W_g = \{w > (0.061,0.085,0.119,0.008)\}$), the fixed matching prior's floor law $1.29\cdot10^{-2}$ per unit of
$1/m - 1/M$ and its margin $+9.9\cdot10^{-3}$ at $w = q$. Globally the loss has its infimum at the saturation: of 120
BFGS runs from random starts one ends at $\vartheta^b(q)$ and 33 below its fit error, all with saturated amplitudes;
the best reaches $6.39\cdot10^{-3}$, and with its two saturated amplitudes held at $\abs{\alpha} = 6$, $8$, $10$, $14$
($\abs{a} = 3.62$--$3.99$) and the rest re-optimised the fit error is $7.14\cdot10^{-3}$, $6.65\cdot10^{-3}$,
$6.49\cdot10^{-3}$, $6.40\cdot10^{-3}$ --- approached, not attained. Globally the servo is
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:27]{Proposition D$'''$.27}'s case (critical points at infinity), and the runs below are
statements about the basin of $\vartheta^b(q)$ and about an engineer's model. A misplaced prior --- the approximant
of the cogging model with the phase 0.5 in place of 0.3, the engineer's model, its own fit error 0.200 --- has a
replicated stationary point (the same from its atoms and from $\vartheta^b(q)$) with the fit error $5.45\cdot10^{-2}$
and the splitting margin $-3.97\cdot10^{-3}$: a D$'''$.5 saddle. (B) The averaged loop ($m = 16$, $64$, $128$ against
$M = 256$, three seeds, 2000 slow units). From warm clouds around $\vartheta^b(q)$ --- computed from the true torque
--- with the matching prior $\vartheta^b(q)$: the fixed prior's nearest-atom leak keeps a finite-size floor
($\norm{\fhat_m - \fhat_M}^2$ $1.85\cdot10^{-3}$, $2.24\cdot10^{-4}$, $4.49\cdot10^{-5}$; fit error 0.174, 0.066,
0.038 against the approximant's 0.0146); the adaptive prior's floor is 38--780 times lower ($\eta = \infty$:
$4.85\cdot10^{-5}$, $2.88\cdot10^{-7}$, $6.36\cdot10^{-8}$), with the fit error 0.0298 (the classes not yet replicated
at $m = 16$ by $s = 2000$), 0.0148, 0.0147; the OT leak toward the matching prior sits at the approximant
(0.01462, no floor); the $\sigma$-modification toward $\vartheta^0 = 0$ at the same $\sigma_0$ keeps a large bias
(0.328) --- and no leak at all ($\sigma_0 = 0$) does as well as the best of them (0.0180, 0.0147, 0.0146; floors
$7.7\cdot10^{-6}$, $2.4\cdot10^{-7}$, $6.6\cdot10^{-8}$). In this noise-free simulation the floors and biases that
the adaptive and OT leaks remove are those a fixed leak creates; the leaks' purpose --- robustness to noise and
disturbances, Propositions \hyperref[res:thmDppp:27]{D$'''$.27}, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{D$'''$.30},
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{D$'''$.31} --- is not exercised here. With
the misplaced prior from that start, at $\eta = \infty$ the prior never acts (its initial position enters only through
the first cells: the runs are the matching ones), at $\eta = \sigma_0$ its bias decays (0.035, 0.020, 0.019 at
$s = 2000$), and the fixed designs keep a bias --- their $m$-systems leave the saddle by splitting their classes
(diameters 0.32--1.07) and end at 0.048 (OT) and 0.10, 0.060, 0.048 (the nearest atom; above the replicated point's
0.0545 at $m = 16$ and $64$). From warm clouds around the engineer's model, with the prior at it --- the start an
engineer has --- the fixed designs stay biased (0.102, 0.060, 0.048; OT 0.048), the adaptive prior removes most of the
bias but slowly ($\eta = \infty$: 0.027, 0.025, 0.022 at $s = 2000$; $\eta = \sigma_0$: 0.032, 0.028, 0.028), and no
leak reaches the basin's fit (0.0152, 0.0147, 0.0147): its $m$ particles spread and explore directions that the
$K_0$-atom structure the leaks impose leaves weakly excited. The collapsed $K_0$-atom flows give the time scale: from
the engineer's model at $\eta = \infty$ --- where the collapsed flow is the unregularised one --- the fit error is
0.0297, 0.0213, 0.0169, 0.0155 at $s = 600$, $2000$, $10^4$, $10^5$, still 0.55 from $\vartheta^b(q)$ in the
parameters ($10^5$ slow units are $2.5\cdot10^6$ time units of the real servo at $k_s = 25$), at $\eta = \sigma_0$
0.0345, 0.0259, 0.0181, 0.0160; from $\vartheta^b(q)$ toward the misplaced prior at $\eta = \sigma_0$ 0.0240, 0.0183,
0.0154, 0.0149. (C) The real servo ($k_s = 25$, RK4 at $\mathrm{d}t = 2\cdot10^{-3}$, 1000 time units, $m = 16$ and
$64$, seed 0) from the oracle-start designs' end states, with each design's leak (its targets refreshed, and the error
recorded, every 0.02 time units; the finite-$\eta$ prior relaxed in real time): the position error's rms is
$1.7$--$2.1\cdot10^{-5}$ for the adaptive designs, the matching OT design and no leak, $7.8\cdot10^{-5}$ for the
misplaced OT design, $9.1\cdot10^{-5}$--$1.8\cdot10^{-4}$ for the fixed nearest-atom prior and
$5.4$--$5.5\cdot10^{-4}$ for the $\sigma$-modification; it equals the prediction of (ii) with the exact filter to
1.0001--1.0029 at $t = 500$ and $1000$ in every design (0.990--1.005 over the first five periods, where the
parameters still move), and with the slaved $s = r/k_s$ to 0.931--0.996, the deficit largest for the adaptive designs,
whose residual is the approximation error's broad fine scale; over these 40 slow units no design drifts, no leak
included. (D) Phase-plane kernels (five isotropic kernels in general position, $\sigma_0 = 0.01$): on the circle 5
exact zero Gauss--Newton eigenvalues (one per kernel) and 8 below $10^{-6}\sigma_0$, the $(K + 1)$-th eigenvalue
$2.4\cdot10^{-10}$ of the largest; on ellipses with the velocity axis scaled by 1.1 and 2, 8 and 6 below
$10^{-6}\sigma_0$ and none exact; on the orbit of $1.2\sin t + 0.3\sin2t$ 3; with the five centres at the origin 16
exact zeros of 20.

% Proposition D'''.30 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-22 version, as committed at
% 29f9394; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/measurement_noise.md). The anchors res:thmDppp:30 and
% res:thmDppp:31 are \hyperref targets, as res:thmDppp:21 is. The references [4] (PARADIGM.md), [S53] and [S57]
% (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) are the bibitems ioannou1996, S53 and S57 of refs.tex.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.30}[measurement noise at perfect tracking: a coherent, high-passed noise, and the
errors in variables]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:30}%
Let \textup{(CL)} be the real loop of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26} ($n = 1$) at the lift $\Gamma_w$
of an exact representation of representable weights $w$, with the prior at its atoms --- fixed there ($\eta = 0$) or
adaptive ($0 < \eta\le\infty$) --- and $\partial_x\fhat = \partial_xf$, $\partial^2_x\fhat = \partial^2_xf$ on the
orbit (an exact representation, an identity of functions, supplies both), and let the controller and the adaptive
law use the measurement $y = x + \nu$ in place of $x$: $u = \dot x_m - k_e(y - x_m) - \fhat_\Theta(y)$,
$\dot\vartheta_i = G\nabla_\vartheta((y - x_m)^\T\tilde\Phi(y;\vartheta_i)) - (\sigma_0/k_e)(\vartheta_i - z_{\pi(i)})$,
$\nu$ a stationary zero-mean noise, independent of the rest, with the spectral density $N_0$ at the frequencies of the
regressor and of the error channel. Let $H := H_L(w) = \langle\hat J^\T\hat J\rangle$ (the whitened weighted
Gauss--Newton matrix, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s $\hat J$) with the eigenpairs $(h_j,v_j)$,
$a(t) := \partial_x\fhat(x_m(t))$, $Q := \langle(\dot{\hat J} + a\hat J)^\T(\dot{\hat J} + a\hat J)\rangle$ and
$Q_{jj} := v_j^\T Qv_j$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(coherence)} The particles of a class see the same $y$, the same error and the same prior atom, so a
    replicated configuration stays replicated under every path of $\nu$: on the replicated subspace the $m$-system is
    the weighted $K_0$-atom loop driven by the same noise, for every $m$ with these weights, at every order in $\nu$.
    Measurement noise does not average out with $m$, as the per-particle dither of Theorem J$'$ does. This is
    invariance, not attraction. A separation $u = G^{-1/2}\delta\vartheta_i$ of class $k$ obeys
    $\dot u = (G^{1/2}\varepsilon_m\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(y;\vartheta_k)G^{1/2} - \sigma_0/k_e)u$ along the
    noisy class trajectory, and to second order in $\nu$ its mean drift is
    $\E[\varepsilon_m^2]G^{1/2}\partial_x\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi G^{1/2}
    + \E[e]G^{1/2}\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi G^{1/2}$ (the measured regressor; the parameters' cross term
    negligible), $\E[e]$ the mean tracking error. At the lift --- the parameters at their atoms, $\E[e]$ the noise's
    own mean error at fixed parameters --- its period average $S_k$ gives the decay rate
    $\sigma_0/k_e - \E\nu^2\lambda_{\max}(S_k)$ to leading order, so near the lift the replicated configuration
    attracts --- and an $m$-system started near it follows the $K_0$-atom loop --- only below the noise level
    $\E\nu^2 = (\sigma_0/k_e)/\max_k\lambda_{\max}(S_k)$. Along the noisy trajectory $\E[e]$ also carries the mean
    tracking error $-\hat J\bar y(t)/k_e$ of the developing errors-in-variables deviation $\bar y(t)$ of (iii), of the
    same order in $\E\nu^2$, so the drift $S_k(t)$ changes on $\bar y$'s time scale and to this order the separation
    follows $\dot u = (\E\nu^2S_k(t) - \sigma_0/k_e)u$ (the first-order multiplicative noise, high-passed by the loop,
    adds a negligible cumulant to its exponent); the long-time threshold --- where $\bar y$ is $O(1)$ at these noise
    levels and the expansion no longer applies --- is open.
  \item \emph{(the floor at first order)} To first order in $\nu$ the measured error's noise is
    $((\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t - a)/(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_e))\nu$ --- the loop high-passes it --- and in the slow
    time the collective coordinates diffuse with the matrix $(N_0/k_e)Q$; their stationary covariance solves
    $\bar A_0\Sigma + \Sigma\bar A_0^\T + (N_0/k_e)\operatorname{diag}(Q,0) = 0$ ($\bar A_0$
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s averaged collective block), and the function-level floor,
    averaged over a period, is
    \[
      \E\norm{\fhat - f}^2_\rho = (N_0/2k_e)\Bigl[\sum_jQ_{jj}(h_j + \eta)/(h_j + \eta + \sigma_0) + \tr H^2\Bigr]
      + O(k_e^{-2})
    \]
    (at time $t$ its fast part is $(N_0/2k_e)\tr(H\hat J(t)^\T\hat J(t))$, whose oscillation over the period is of
    the floor's own order): the parameters' part, each mode's share multiplied by
    $(h_j + \eta)/(h_j + \eta + \sigma_0)$ --- the fixed prior's Tikhonov filter factor $h_j/(h_j + \sigma_0)$ at
    $\eta = 0$, the factor 1 at $\eta = \infty$, where the part is $(N_0/2k_e)\tr Q$ whatever the spectrum --- and
    the jitter of the frequencies that the loop passes, independent of the prior (the compensation error at the
    plant's current point has $\langle\abs{\hat J}^4\rangle$ in place of $\tr H^2$). Mode $j$'s parameter variance is
    $(N_0/2k_e)Q_{jj}(h_j + \eta)/(h_j(h_j + \eta + \sigma_0))$ --- from $Q_{jj}/(2(h_j + \sigma_0))$ to
    $Q_{jj}/(2h_j)$ in units of $N_0/k_e$ --- plus the fast part $(N_0/2k_e)h_j$.
  \item \emph{(the errors in variables)} To second order the noise inside the regressor $\tilde\Phi(y)$ exerts a mean
    force on the collective coordinates. Its part carried by the noise's own second moments, at fixed parameters, is
    $k_eF$ in the slow time with $F := \langle\hat J'^\T\E[\varepsilon_m^2] + \hat J^\T\E[e]\rangle = O(\E\nu^2)$
    ($\hat J' := \partial_x\hat J$, $\varepsilon_m := y - x_m$ the measured error; for an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck noise of
    rate $B$, $\E[\varepsilon_m^2] = \E\nu^2(a^2 + k_eB)/(k_e(k_e + B))\to\E\nu^2$ as $B/k_e\to\infty$); the
    parameters' first-order fluctuation adds a force of the same order in $N_0$ --- the compensation's curvature and
    slope--parameter terms in the error equation, the correlation
    $\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi\E[\varepsilon_m\delta\vartheta]$ in the law --- negligible against $F$ for the
    measured regressor, not for the desired one (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}(iv)). The
    stationary response is $k_e(H + \sigma_0I)^{-1}F$ under the fixed prior and $k_eH^{-1}F$ under the adaptive
    prior, for every $\eta > 0$: the adaptive prior gives up the leak's bound on the response to a persistent force
    in the weakly excited modes --- the robustness half of the classical trade-off, here against the noise, and the
    lack-of-excitation drift of adaptive control (\cite{S57}) --- and its function-level bias $k_e^2F^\T H^{-1}F$
    grows like $k_e^2(\E\nu^2)^2$ while the noise is white well beyond the loop's bandwidth ($k_e\ll B$), and
    saturates for $k_e\gg B$, where $\E[\varepsilon_m^2]\to\E\nu^2B/k_e$. The part of $F$ that the adaptive prior
    amplifies is the regressor's own, $\langle\hat J'^\T\E[\varepsilon_m^2]\rangle$; evaluating the regressor at the
    reference removes it (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:31]{Proposition D$'''$.31}).
\end{enumerate}
Hence $\eta$ buys the bias-removal rate $\lambda_j^-(\eta)$ at the price of the factor
$(h_j + \eta)/(h_j + \eta + \sigma_0)$ in the floor and of the $H^{-1}$ response to the errors in variables, and $k_e$
lowers the floor like $1/k_e$ while it raises the errors-in-variables bias like $k_e^2$ ($k_e\ll B$).
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) The equations of the particles of a class are identical --- the same $y$, the same measured error and the same
prior atom (the class mean at $\eta = \infty$, the same $z_k$ otherwise) enter them --- and so are their initial
values; the field is locally Lipschitz and the cells are constant near the lift, so the trajectories coincide, and the
class means follow the weighted $K_0$-atom loop with the same noise. (ii) Linearised at $\Gamma_w$ in
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s whitened coordinates:
$\delta\dot e = -k_e\delta e - \hat Jy - (k_e + a)\nu$ (from $f(x) - \fhat(x + \nu) = -D\fhat[\delta\Theta] - a\nu$),
$\dot y = \hat J^\T(\delta e + \nu) - (\sigma_0/k_e)(y - \zeta)$, $\dot\zeta = -(\eta/k_e)(\zeta - y)$ (the
regressor's own noise is of second order). The measured error $\varepsilon_m = \delta e + \nu$ obeys
$\dot\varepsilon_m = -k_e\varepsilon_m - \hat Jy + \dot\nu - a\nu$, so
$\varepsilon_m = -(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_e)^{-1}[\hat Jy] + n$,
$n := (\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_e)^{-1}[\dot\nu - a\nu]$ (written
$((\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t - a)/(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_e))\nu$): the first part is
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s slaving, the second drives $\dot y = \hat J^\T n$. With
$\xi := (\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_e)^{-1}\nu$, of variance $N_0/(2k_e)$ for white noise,
$n = \dot\xi - a\xi + (\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}t + k_e)^{-1}[\dot a\xi]$ and
$\hat J^\T n = \mathrm{d}(\hat J^\T\xi)/\mathrm{d}t - (\dot{\hat J} + a\hat J)^\T\xi + O(\xi/k_e)$:
$y = \hat J^\T\xi + u$, the fast jitter $\hat J^\T\xi$ and the slow part $u$ driven by
$-(\dot{\hat J} + a\hat J)^\T\xi$, white at the slow scale with the intensity $(N_0/k_e^2)Q$ per unit time,
$(N_0/k_e)Q$ in the slow time; the averaged slow equation
$\dd Y = \bar A_0Y\dd s + (N_0/k_e)^{1/2}(Q^{1/2}\dd W,0)$ (stochastic averaging, \cite{S53}; exactly, for the
linearised loop, \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26a]{Lemma D$'''$.26a}(v)) has the stated stationary covariance. In the
eigenbasis of $H$ the block $\bar A_0$ is a direct sum of the pair blocks
$\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-(h + \sigma_0) & \sigma_0\\ \eta & -\eta\end{smallmatrix}\right]$ ($\eta = 0$:
$-(h + \sigma_0)$; $\eta = \infty$: $-h$), whose Lyapunov equation with the noise $q$ on $y$ gives
$\Sigma_{\zeta\zeta} = \Sigma_{y\zeta} = \eta\Sigma_{yy}/(h + \eta)$ and
$\Sigma_{yy} = q(h + \eta)/(2h(h + \eta + \sigma_0))$; $Q$'s off-diagonal entries in that basis enter only the
covariances of different modes, which $\tr(H\Sigma_{yy}) = \sum_jh_j\Sigma_{yy,jj}$ does not see. The fast jitter
gives $\E[(\hat J^\T\xi)^\T H(\hat J^\T\xi)] = (N_0/2k_e)\tr H^2$ over a period, and
$\E[(\hat J(t)\hat J(t)^\T\xi)^2] = (N_0/2k_e)\langle\abs{\hat J}^4\rangle$. (iii) At fixed parameters the
second-order part of $\E[\varepsilon_m\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(y;\vartheta)]$ at the lift is
$\partial_x\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi\cdot\E[\varepsilon_m^2] + \nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi\cdot\E[e]$, $\E[e]$ from the
error equation's second-order terms $\frac12\partial^2_xf\cdot e^2 - \frac12\partial^2_x\fhat\cdot(e + \nu)^2$ --- one
second derivative for $f$ and $\fhat$, by the hypothesis (otherwise
$\frac12(\partial^2_xf - \partial^2_x\fhat)\operatorname{Var}(e)$ remains, of the same order); the parameters'
fluctuation adds $\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi\cdot\E[\varepsilon_m\delta\vartheta]$ in the law and
$-\partial_xD\fhat[\E[(\delta e + \nu)\delta\Theta]] - \frac12\E[D^2_\Theta\fhat[\delta\Theta,\delta\Theta]]$ in the
error equation, of the same order in $N_0$ (computed in the numerics from the periodic covariance: the full
second-order mean). For the Ornstein--Uhlenbeck noise, quasi-statically along the orbit,
$\E[\nu e] = -(k_e + a)\E\nu^2/(k_e + B)$ and $\operatorname{Var}(e) = (k_e + a)^2\E\nu^2/(k_e(k_e + B))$, which give
$\E[\varepsilon_m^2] = \E\nu^2 + 2\E[\nu e] + \operatorname{Var}(e)$ as stated and
$\E[e] = -(\partial^2_x\fhat/(2k_e))\E\nu^2(B - k_e - 2a)/(k_e + B)$. A constant slow force $F_s$ on the pair block of
a mode has the stationary point $\zeta = y$ and $(h + \sigma_0)y - \sigma_0\zeta = F_s$, i.e.\ $y = F_s/h$ for every
$\eta > 0$, and $y = F_s/(h + \sigma_0)$ under the fixed prior.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved, exactly, as invariance; the threshold formal at the lift (the separations' averaged second-order
drift there), the drift along the noisy trajectory computed from the second-order transient and checked against the
real loop's separation rates on antithetic noise pairs, the long-time threshold open; (ii) proved for the linearised
loop under white noise, for the floor's period average (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26a]{Lemma D$'''$.26a}(v): the slow
coordinates' drift decoupled exactly, their noise $\varepsilon b\nu$, at leading order the high-passed one; at
$\eta = 0$ with $K = \hat J$, $\Lambda = \sigma_0I$), with the relative remainder $O(1/k_e)$, the closed form exact for
the averaged system; the OU noise of the numerics and the real loop formal --- and checked against the exact periodic
covariance of the linearised loop: the deficit 2.4\%, 0.61\%, 0.14\%, 0.028\% at $k_e = 25$--$200$ ($\eta = 0$) is
dominated there by a term in $k_e^{-2}$, but the relative error is $O(1/k_e)$ --- the slope $a$ in the noise input
$-(k_e + a)\nu$, a rate that does not change sign under time reversal, breaks the parity of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}(c) (with the noise input $-k_e\nu$ the deviation is numerically even in
$1/k_e$, to $k_e = 800$) --- and the ratio crosses 1 near $k_e = 500$; (iii) formal --- a quasi-static second-moment
computation for the noise's own moments, the parameters' part computed numerically --- its stationary response exact
for the averaged system, the exact linearised loop's 10.7\% (adaptive) and 2.6\% (fixed) higher at $k_e = 25$, an
$O(k_e^{-2})$ excess; the real nonlinear loop checked by Monte Carlo against the sum of the two. Classical:
measurement noise in adaptive control and the errors-in-variables bias of estimates from noisy regressors
(bias-compensated least squares and instrumental variables in identification; bounded disturbances and robust
modifications, \cite{ioannou1996}); using the reference in the regressor, as the desired compensation adaptation law
does (\cite{S53}); the Lyapunov covariance of a constant-gain adaptive algorithm as its diffusion approximation
(Benveniste--M\'etivier--Priouret), the LMS misadjustment's dependence on the input's spectrum through a trace only
(Widrow et al.: the $\eta = \infty$ floor's $\tr Q$), the leaky LMS's steady state (Mayyas--Aboulnasr: the fixed
prior's factor $h_j/(h_j + \sigma_0)$), the common noise of mean-field theory (Carmona--Delarue--Lacker), of which the
coherence is an instance, and the drift of the weakly excited directions under a persistent disturbance (Anderson), of
which the $H^{-1}$ response is (\cite{S57}). New, in the transport-leak setting: the interpolation
$(h_j + \eta)/(h_j + \eta + \sigma_0)$ of the floor in the prior's mobility, the driving matrix
$Q = \langle(\dot{\hat J} + a\hat J)^\T(\dot{\hat J} + a\hat J)\rangle$ --- the sensitivity-filtered measurement the
law sees, built on \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s derivative Gram --- and their combination with the
coherence of the $K_0$-atom identification and its noise threshold at the lift. Checked:
\code{tests/test_measurement_noise.py} (the closed form against the Lyapunov solution, the trace identity at
$\eta = \infty$ and the monotonicity in $\eta$; the exact periodic floors of the linearised loop at $k_e = 100$ for
$\eta = 0$, $\sigma_0$ and $\infty$; a lift $m = 6$ under a noise path staying exactly replicated and following the
$K_0$-atom loop driven by the same path; a separation of the weakest class decaying at the noise std 0.01 and growing
at 0.05 --- a sign test, the rates within $10^{-4}$ of the lift's prediction ---; the separations' drift against an
independent quadrature of its two moments, and along the second-order transient the mean parameters moved by the
averaged force and the mean error carrying the tracking error of the developing deviation; the OU noise's stationary
variance and correlation, and the law seeing the measured error at the measured point; the responses
$(H + \sigma_0I)^{-1}$ and $H^{-1}$ to a constant force, the quasi-static force against the full second-order mean's
noise part to $2\cdot10^{-3}$, the parameters' part below $10^{-4}$ of the bias; the exact periodic mean against the
filtered-Gram response under both priors, the fixed prior's branch and its leak in the bias; and the mean function's
curvature term against an independent contraction).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.30 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/measurement_noise.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_measurement_noise.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/measurement_noise.md}). The
three-bump instance ($\sigma_0 = 0.15\sigma_{\mathrm{crit}}$, $x_m = 2\sin t$, $k_e = 25$) at the lift of the exact
representation of the matching weights $q = 1/3$, the prior at its atoms. (A) The linear theory: $H$'s nine eigenvalues
span $3.06\cdot10^{-4}$--$0.195$; $\tr Q = 8.784$, of which 8.105 is the derivative Gram
$\langle\dot{\hat J}^\T\dot{\hat J}\rangle$ and 0.679 the slope term $\langle a^2\hat J^\T\hat J\rangle$ (the cross
term vanishes, as on every one-dimensional reference: an exact differential); $Q_{jj}/h_j$ ranges from 104 (mode 1) to
5.5 (mode 6). The parameters' floor per unit $N_0$ is 0.1597 under the fixed prior, 0.1627 at $\eta = \sigma_0$,
0.1690 at $10\sigma_0$ and $0.1757 = \tr Q/(2k_e)$ at $\eta = \infty$ --- the adaptive prior costs 10\% here, the
strong modes carrying the floor --- while the weakest mode's parameter variance grows from 0.0865 to 2.09,
$\times24.1$ in the averaged system ($\times28.0$ in the exact linearised loop at $k_e = 25$, Part B). The fast parts
are $\tr H^2 = 0.0657$ and $\langle\abs{\hat J}^4\rangle = 0.299$. The second-order force of the noise's own moments
(OU noise, $B = 100$) has the averaged function-level stationary bias 491 (fixed prior) and 2397 (adaptive,
$\times4.88$) per unit $(\E\nu^2)^2$, equal to the floor of the parameters at the noise std 0.0026 and 0.0012
respectively and dominant above; the full second-order mean (the periodic covariance contracted with the true field's
second derivatives) reproduces the force to $1.7\cdot10^{-4}$, the parameters' fluctuation changing the bias by less
than $10^{-5}$ ($2397.66\to2397.68$; the mean function's 2397.65), and the exact periodic second-order mean of the
linearised loop gives 504 and 2653 ($\times5.27$). The averaged bias grows in $k_e$ with the local exponent 1.93--1.99
for $B\ge20k_e$ but 1.69 and 1.47 at $B = 8k_e$ and $4k_e$, and saturates beyond. At the lift the separations'
second-order drift has $\lambda_{\max}(S_k) = 0.0148$, $0.0101$ and $0.298$ per unit $\E\nu^2$ (real time) in the
three classes: there the third class's separations stop decaying at the noise std 0.031 (1.5\% of the reference's
amplitude), and at Part C's stds 0.003 and 0.01 the margin $\sigma_0/k_e$ is used to 0.9\% and 10.5\%. (B) The exact
periodic stationary covariance of the linearised loop (the monodromy and a discrete Lyapunov equation) against the
prediction, at $\eta = 0$, $\sigma_0$, $\infty$ and $k_e = 25$--$800$: the function-level floor's ratio 0.976--0.980
at $k_e = 25$, 0.994--0.995 at 50, 0.9986--0.9988 at 100, 0.9997--0.9998 at 200, and above 1 at 800
($+1.2\cdot10^{-5}$ at $\eta = 0$, $+1.3\cdot10^{-5}$ at $\eta = \infty$); the deviation's successive ratios per
doubling are 3.87, 4.38, 5.26, 11.4 and a change of sign ($\eta = \infty$) --- a $k_e^{-2}$ term dominating to
$k_e = 200$ over a first-order one of the opposite sign --- while with the noise input $-k_e\nu$ (the slope removed,
the control) they are 3.67, 3.90, 3.98, 3.99, 4.00: without the slope the expansion is numerically even (to
$k_e = 800$; not proved). The weakest mode's variance is 0.845 (fixed prior) and 0.980 (adaptive) of its
slow-plus-fast prediction at $k_e = 25$, tending to 1. The exact stationary second-order mean of the linearised loop
lies 10.7\%, 2.65\%, 0.66\%, 0.17\% above the averaged bias under the adaptive prior and 2.58\%, 0.64\%, 0.16\%,
0.04\% under the fixed one at $k_e = 25$, $50$, $100$, $200$ --- the weak modes' stiffness being the filtered Gram
$H - k_e^{-2}\langle\dot{\hat J}^\T\dot{\hat J}\rangle$ of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}(c). (C) The
real loop (RK4, $\mathrm{d}t = 10^{-3}$, an OU noise exact at the steps, 12 noise paths of 300 time units common to the
four cases) against the transient covariance of the linearised loop plus the second-order mean deviation: at the noise
std 0.003 within one standard error at every record ($z$ from $-0.99$ to $0.99$), the mean part 42\% of the
prediction at $t = 300$; at std 0.01 within 1.75 standard errors ($z$ from $-0.3$ to $1.75$, the records of a run
correlated), the mean part 89\% at $t = 300$ and the covariance part alone 8.8--8.9 times below the Monte Carlo ---
the second-order force is the dominant effect of the noise there. The adaptive and the fixed prior drift apart, by
6.0\% and 6.4\% at $t = 300$ (predicted 5.3\% and 6.1\%): the weak modes, where the two responses part, equilibrate
over $k_e/h_1\approx8\cdot10^4$ time units, so over 300 time units the transient checks the force (the weak modes
still grow linearly), and the stationary response is checked against the exact linearised loop (B). The lift
$m = 15$ (classes of five) driven by the same noise path as the $K_0$-atom run gives the same $\fhat$ to rounding
(identical in the stored float32, $5.6\cdot10^{-16}$ apart in float64): the noise is coherent, as (i) states. (D) But
invariance is not attraction: from a separation of size $10^{-6}$ along $S_3$'s top eigenvector, on eight antithetic
pairs of noise paths of 300 time units per std ($\nu$ and $-\nu$, whose pair means cancel the first-order part of the
log-growth), the lift's third class separates at $-2.50\cdot10^{-4}\pm3.8\cdot10^{-7}$ per time unit at the std
0.01, $+2.74\cdot10^{-6}\pm3.3\cdot10^{-6}$ at 0.03 and $+4.53\cdot10^{-4}\pm8.4\cdot10^{-6}$ at 0.05. The
noise-induced part $\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}} := (\mathrm{rate} + \sigma_0/k_e)/\E\nu^2$ is $0.3271\pm0.0038$,
$0.3175\pm0.0037$ and $0.2943\pm0.0034$, against the lift's drift $\lambda_{\max}(S_3) = 0.2981$ and the drift along
the second-order transient from the lift, 0.3264, 0.3270 and 0.3281 (the same estimator applied to
$\dot u = (\E\nu^2S_3(t) - \sigma_0/k_e)u$). Along the transient the mean error's rms per unit $\E\nu^2$ grows from
0.121 to 0.602 over the six 50-unit windows to $t = 300$ and $\lambda_{\max}(S_3(t))$ from 0.307 to 0.349: the
tracking error of the developing errors-in-variables deviation. The weighted fit
$\lambda_{\mathrm{eff}} = c_0 + c_1\E\nu^2$ has the small-noise limit $c_0 = 0.3291\pm0.0033$ --- 10.4\% above the
lift's drift, within its standard error of the transient's 0.3264 --- and the next order $c_1 = -13.8\pm2.0$; over
this window the measured rate vanishes at the std 0.0299, against 0.0308 by the lift's drift and 0.0294 by the
transient's. Above it the replicated configuration repels, its within-class spread staying exactly 0.0 from a
replicated start only because each particle's update is the same arithmetic.
\end{sloppypar}

% Proposition D'''.31 with its proof, Status and numerics (PARADIGM.md section 5d, round-22 version, as committed at
% 29f9394; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md). The references [S49], [S53], [S54],
% [S57] and [S58] (LITERATURE_TRANSPORT.md) are the bibitems S49, S53, S54, S57 and S58 of refs.tex.
\begin{thm}{Proposition}{D$'''$.31}[the adaptive prior is a washout leak; the desired regressor makes the noise's
persistent force a least-squares force]\phantomsection\label{res:thmDppp:31}%
Let the leak be \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}'s toward an adaptive prior,
$\dd z_j/\dd s = -\eta(z_j - \hat T_j)$ with $0 < \eta < \infty$ (the nearest-atom form of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:24]{Proposition D$'''$.24}(ii) with the class barycentres likewise), in the averaged loop or in
the real loop \textup{(CL)} of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26} ($s = t/k_e$); at an exact
representation of representable weights $w$ let $H$, $\hat J$, $a$ and the mean $\langle\cdot\rangle$ over a period be
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s, $\norm{g}^2_\rho := \langle g^2\rangle$ for a function $g$ along the
orbit, and $P$ the $L^2_\rho$-projection onto the span of $\hat J$'s components.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
  \item \emph{(the washout)} Along every solution and every path of the plan with $\hat T_j$ of bounded variation,
    $\hat T_j(s) - z_j(s) = \int_0^se^{-\eta(s-\tau)}\dd\hat T_j(\tau) + e^{-\eta s}(\hat T_j(0) - z_j(0))$ --- and for
    every measurable $\hat T_j$ (a chattering plan included) in the integrated form
    $z_j(s) = e^{-\eta s}z_j(0) + \eta\int_0^se^{-\eta(s-\tau)}\hat T_j(\tau)\dd\tau$: the prior lags the barycentre by
    the barycentre's exponentially forgotten increments. For integral plans (the nearest-atom cells, the OT plan at a
    commensurate $m$) the leak on a particle, $-\sigma_0(\vartheta_i - z)$, is therefore the full
    $\sigma$-modification of its offset from the barycentre plus, on the barycentre, the $\sigma$-modification passed
    through the washout filter $(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}s)(\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}s + \eta)^{-1}$ --- zero at zero
    frequency, the full leak above $\eta$; with split rows (an incommensurate $m$) the barycentres also feel
    $-\sigma_0(I - M)z$, $M_{jl} := (m/q_j)\sum_i\gamma_{ij}\gamma_{il}$, which is not washed out. Linearised at the
    exact representation, mode $j$ of the collective block has at the slow frequency $\omega$ the stiffness
    $h_j + \sigma_0i\omega/(i\omega + \eta)$: the fixed prior's $h_j + \sigma_0$ for $\omega\gg\eta$, the bare $h_j$ at
    $\omega = 0$. This is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:23]{Theorem D$'''$.23}(iii)'s bias removal --- feedback through a
    washout filter leaves the equilibria where they are (\cite{S54}), here the replicated ones --- and its price: no
    leak opposes a persistent force on the class barycentres (the separations keep the full leak).
  \item \emph{(the stationary identity)} Let the prior be an affine time-invariant filter of the barycentre and of a
    fixed anchor $z_a$ --- linearised, $\zeta = \mathcal{K}[y] + (I - \mathcal{K})[\delta]$ in the whitened weighted
    coordinates, $\delta$ the anchor's misplacement from the representation; for example
    $\dd z/\dd s = -\eta(z - \hat T) - \kappa(z - z_a)$, $\kappa\ge0$, with $\mathcal{K}(0) = \eta/(\eta + \kappa)$
    ($\kappa = 0$ the adaptive prior, $\kappa = \infty$ the fixed prior at $z_a$) --- and
    $S_0 := \sigma_0(I - \mathcal{K}(0))$ the leak's zero-frequency gain (the anchor entering through
    $I - \mathcal{K}$: the filter returns the anchor when the barycentre sits there). A persistent force $F$ on the
    collective coordinates and the misplacement $\delta$ give the stationary deviation $y = RF + B\delta$ with
    $R = (H + S_0)^{-1}$ and $B = (H + S_0)^{-1}S_0$, so
    \[
      RH + B = I,\qquad\text{i.e.}\qquad R = (I - B)H^{-1}:
    \]
    the response is the unregularised one times the share not taken by the anchor ($HR + B = I$ as well where $S_0$
    commutes with $H$, as for the family; not for a prior fixed on some coordinates or a projected prior with an
    active face). On every mode, whatever $S_0$, $h_jr_{jj} + b_{jj} = 1$ ($r_{jj} := v_j^\T Rv_j$,
    $b_{jj} := v_j^\T Bv_j$): a vanishing bias forces the response $1/h_j$. So no such prior both removes the leak's
    bias and keeps its bound on the response to persistent forces --- the trade binding on the modes with
    $h_j < \sigma_0$, where $1/h_j$ exceeds the fixed prior's $1/\sigma_0$ --- and a remedy for the robustness loss of
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(iii) has to change the force.
  \item \emph{(least-squares forces)} For $H\succ0$ and every $S$ with $S + S^\T\succeq0$,
    $F^\T(H + S)^{-\T}H(H + S)^{-1}F\le F^\T H^{-1}F = \min\{\norm{g}^2_\rho : \langle\hat J^\T g\rangle = F\}$, with
    equality at $S = 0$: the function-level stationary response to a persistent force is at most the squared norm of
    the smallest target perturbation that exerts it ($\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$ is the learning force of the target
    $f + g$), and the adaptive prior attains the bound. A least-squares force $\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$ therefore
    moves the learned function by at most $\norm{g}_\rho$ under every prior --- under the adaptive prior by exactly
    $Pg$, so that the stationary point's projected residual $P(g - \hat Jy)$ vanishes.
  \item \emph{(the desired regressor)} Let the adaptive law evaluate its regressor at the reference,
    $\dot\vartheta_i = G\nabla_\vartheta((y - x_m)^\T\tilde\Phi(x_m;\vartheta_i))
    - (\sigma_0/k_e)(\vartheta_i - z_{\pi(i)})$ (DR), with the compensation $\fhat_\Theta(y)$ as before or also at
    the reference, $u = \dot x_m - k_e(y - x_m) - \fhat_\Theta(x_m)$ (DR*, the desired compensation adaptation
    law~\cite{S53}). Then (a) without noise the lift $\Gamma_w$ is a solution and the linearisation at it is
    \hyperref[res:thmDppp:26]{Corollary D$'''$.26}'s --- for DR exactly (the regressor's variation multiplies
    $e\equiv0$), for DR* with $-(k_e - a(t))$ in place of $-k_e$ in the error channel --- and the averaged loop is
    unchanged at leading order in $1/k_e$ (the regressor moves by $x - x_m = O(1/k_e)$); (b) to first order in $\nu$,
    DR is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(ii)'s linearised loop driven by the same noise, path by
    path; DR* has the noise input $-k_e\nu$ in place of $-(k_e + a)\nu$, and the same floor up to a relative
    $O(1/k_e)$; (c) to second order the part of the force carried by the noise's own second moments (at fixed
    parameters) is the least-squares force $\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$ (slow time) of $g := k_e\E[e]$, the rescaled
    mean tracking error at the lift; for an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck noise of rate $B$, quasi-statically along the orbit,
    $g = -\frac12\partial^2_x\fhat\cdot\E\nu^2(B - k_e - 2a)/(k_e + B)$ for DR and
    $g = \frac12\partial^2_xf\cdot\E\nu^2k_e^3/((k_e - a)^2(k_e - a + B))$ for DR* ($k_e > \max a$), so
    $\abs{g}\le\frac12\abs{\partial^2_xf}\E\nu^2(1 + O(\abs{a}/k_e))$ for all $k_e$ and $B$. By (iii) its
    function-level response is at most
    $\norm{g}^2_\rho\le\frac14\norm{\partial^2_xf}^2_\rho(\E\nu^2)^2(1 + O(1/k_e))$ under every prior --- uniformly in
    $k_e$, in $B$ and in the spectrum of $H$ --- and under the adaptive prior it leaves no projected mean tracking
    error. The parameters' first-order fluctuation adds a second-order force of the same order in $N_0$: in the error
    equation the compensation's curvature $\frac12D^2_\Theta\fhat[\Sigma_{\Theta\Theta}]$ and, for MR and DR, its
    slope--parameter term (DR*'s compensation, at $x_m$, has none) --- a least-squares force again, of a perturbation
    set by the parameters' covariance; the curvature also shifts the mean function, and the loop removes its tangent
    part ---, in the learning law the correlation $\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi\E[\varepsilon_m\delta\vartheta]$ of
    the regressor's parameter dependence with the measured error --- an errors-in-variables term in the parameters,
    which the reference in the regressor does not remove and which is not the learning force of a mean tracking
    error, so no bound in the noise's moments applies to it. Its size is set by the parameters' stationary covariance
    (mode $j$'s variance $(N_0/2k_e)Q_{jj}(h_j + \eta)/(h_j(h_j + \eta + \sigma_0))$, unbounded as $h_j\to0$ at
    $\eta > 0$), so the bound $\norm{g}^2_\rho$ is the noise part's, not the total's. With the measured regressor the
    noise part is $F_1 + \langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$, $F_1 := k_e\langle\hat J'^\T\E[\varepsilon_m^2]\rangle$ --- the
    regressor's errors in variables proper, the learning force of no mean tracking error --- and the adaptive prior's
    stationary point keeps the projected mean tracking error $P\E[e] = -\hat JH^{-1}F_1/k_e$: what grows like $k_e^2$
    while $k_e\ll B$ is $k_e$ times it, of squared norm $F_1^\T H^{-1}F_1$, the function-level bias; the mean tracking
    error itself, of squared norm $F_1^\T H^{-1}F_1/k_e^2\propto(B/(k_e + B))^2$, stays bounded.
\end{enumerate}
Hence with the desired regressor the first-order floor still falls like $1/k_e$ while the noise part of the
errors-in-variables bias stays bounded, and on the instance the parameters' part's own response falls like $k_e^{-2}$
--- a one-dimensional reference, where the law's leading term, the period average of a function of $x_m$ times
$\dot x_m$ (a line integral $\oint M\partial_x\hat J\dd x$ along the orbit), vanishes; on a curve in $\R^n$, $n > 1$,
it need not: the trade-off in $k_e$ of \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30} disappears, and what is left is
the plant's state jitter $\E[e^2] = \E\nu^2(k_e + a)^2/(k_e(k_e + B))$ (DR; DR*:
$k_e^2\E\nu^2/((k_e - a)(k_e - a + B))$) --- the classical price of a high gain under measurement noise.
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
(i) The integrated form is the variation of constants for $\dd z_j/\dd s = -\eta(z_j - \hat T_j)$; for $\hat T_j$ of
bounded variation (it jumps where the plan does, finitely often on bounded intervals), $u := \hat T_j - z_j$ satisfies
$\dd u = \dd\hat T_j - \eta u\dd s$, whence $u(s) = e^{-\eta s}u(0) + \int_0^se^{-\eta(s-\tau)}\dd\hat T_j(\tau)$; and
$\vartheta_i - z = (\vartheta_i - \hat T) + u$ for an integral plan (for split rows the targets are
$m\sum_j\gamma_{ij}z_j$, and averaging the leak over a class gives $-\sigma_0(\hat T - Mz)$). Linearised,
$\zeta = \eta(i\omega + \eta)^{-1}y$ on each mode (the prior and the leak are scalars in the whitened weighted
coordinates, so they commute with $H$), and $\sigma_0(y - \zeta) = \sigma_0i\omega(i\omega + \eta)^{-1}y$. (ii) At
zero frequency $\zeta = \mathcal{K}(0)y + (I - \mathcal{K}(0))\delta$, so the leak on $y$ is
$-\sigma_0(y - \zeta) = -S_0(y - \delta)$, and the stationary equation $Hy + S_0(y - \delta) = F$ gives
$y = (H + S_0)^{-1}(F + S_0\delta)$; then $RH + B = (H + S_0)^{-1}(H + S_0) = I$, and on the diagonal in $H$'s
eigenbasis $h_jr_{jj} + b_{jj} = 1$. For the family, the prior's stationary equation
$\eta(y - \zeta) = \kappa(\zeta - \delta)$ gives $\mathcal{K}(0) = \eta/(\eta + \kappa)$. (iii) By congruence with
$H + S$, $(H + S)^{-\T}H(H + S)^{-1}\le H^{-1}\Leftrightarrow H\le(H + S)^\T H^{-1}(H + S)
= H + S + S^\T + S^\T H^{-1}S$, true for $S + S^\T\succeq0$. The map $g\mapsto\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$ has the
adjoint $F\mapsto\hat JF$, so the smallest $g$ with $\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle = F$ is $\hat JH^{-1}F$, of squared norm
$F^\T H^{-1}F$; for $F = \langle\hat J^\T g\rangle$, $F^\T H^{-1}F = \norm{Pg}^2_\rho\le\norm{g}^2_\rho$, and at
$S = 0$, $\hat Jy = \hat JH^{-1}\langle\hat J^\T g\rangle = Pg$. (iv) (a) At $e = 0$ both laws vanish, and their
variations, $\delta e\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x;\vartheta) + e(\cdots)$, coincide at $x = x_m$; for DR*,
$f(x) - \fhat(x_m) = a\delta e - \hat Jy$ to first order. Off the lift $e$ is slaved,
$e = r(x_m)/k_e + O(k_e^{-2})$, so the two regressors differ by $O(1/k_e)$. (b) The regressor's dependence on $y$
enters the law multiplied by $\varepsilon_m$, at second order; for DR* the compensation no longer carries $\nu$. (c) At
fixed parameters the mean of $\varepsilon_m\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m;\vartheta)$ is
$\nabla_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m)\E[e]$, since $\E\nu = 0$; the parameters' fluctuation adds
$\nabla^2_\vartheta\tilde\Phi(x_m)\E[\varepsilon_m\delta\vartheta]$ in the law and the compensation's curvature and
slope--parameter terms in the error equation (\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s proof). For DR,
$\E[e]$ is \hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}'s (the same compensation); for DR*,
$\dot e = (a - k_e)e + \frac12\partial^2_xf\,e^2 - k_e\nu + O(3)$, whose linear part has
$\operatorname{Var}(e) = k_e^2\E\nu^2/((k_e - a)(k_e - a + B))$ for the OU noise, and the mean balance is
$(k_e - a)\E[e] = \frac12\partial^2_xf\operatorname{Var}(e)$. The bounds:
$\abs{B - k_e - 2a}\le(k_e + B)(1 + 2\abs{a}/(k_e + B))$ and
$k_e^3/((k_e - a)^2(k_e - a + B))\le(k_e/(k_e - a))^3$. With the measured regressor, at the adaptive prior's
stationary point $\langle\hat J^\T(g - \hat Jy)\rangle + F_1 = 0$ and the mean of the error equation gives
$k_e\E[e] = g - \hat Jy$ at second order, so $k_eP\E[e] = -\hat JH^{-1}F_1$.
\end{proof}
\status{(i) proved, exactly (every solution of the real or the averaged loop; the differential form for $\hat T$ of
bounded variation, the integrated one always; the leak's decomposition for integral plans); (ii) proved as
$RH + B = I$ and per mode --- linear algebra on the averaged linearised block; $HR + B = I$ where $S_0$ commutes with
$H$; (iii) proved ($S + S^\T\succeq0$, $H\succ0$); (iv)(a) proved at the lift and for its linearisation, the
leading-order equality of the averaged loops by Theorem J's averaging argument (not rewritten); (iv)(b) the
first-order identity proved for the linearised equations, the floors themselves with
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(ii)'s status; (iv)(c) formal and split --- the noise part by
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30}(iii)'s quasi-static second-moment computation, its consequences exact
for the averaged system; the parameters' part computed numerically (the full second-order mean), not in closed form.
Classical: reference regressors and the desired compensation adaptation law (\cite{S53}; DR* without Sadegh and
Horowitz's nonlinear damping is a local statement), washout filters (\cite{S54}), the adaptive law kept near a
low-pass filtered copy of itself --- Yucelen and Haddad's low-frequency learning, which has the washout structure
(\cite{S58}) --- and elastic averaging's moving-average centre (\cite{S49}); the identity, per mode the Tikhonov
filter factor plus the residual factor (the Kalman-gain split of a regularised estimate); the bound (iii), the
minimum-norm solution with the contraction of the regularised fit; and the split of the noise's force into the
regressor's errors in variables proper and the output's, of which only the mean biases; and the parameters' own errors
in variables, the classical second-order bias of a constant-gain stochastic approximation from its own fluctuation ---
the mean field's curvature in the parameters contracted with their stationary covariance, plus the noise--parameter
correlation (Benveniste--M\'etivier--Priouret; for constant-step stochastic gradient descent Dieuleveut--Durmus--Bach,
who remove it by Richardson--Romberg extrapolation; the curvature bias of nonlinear least squares, Box's, the analogue
of $\frac12D^2_\Theta\fhat[\Sigma_{\Theta\Theta}]$: \cite{S57}). New are their transfer to the transport leaks --- the
washout reading of the adaptive prior in the mass-constrained, mean-field setting, uniform in $m$ --- and the
instance's quantification, with the observation that the desired regressor does not remove the parameters' part.
Checked: \code{tests/test_eiv_remedy.py} (the washout identity along a real-loop solution at finite $\eta$, the
barycentre's velocity integrated alongside, and on the averaged loop across a plan switch, the jump's share large; the
collective block's transfer function against the washout formula, $RH + B = I$ along the prior family and, $R$ and
$B$ from their dynamic blocks, for priors whose zero-frequency leak does not commute with $H$ --- fixed on the
amplitudes, one active face, a matrix anchor gain, a random one with $K + K^\T\succeq0$ --- where $HR + B = I$ fails;
the response bound under random leaks $S\ge0$, the smallest equivalent perturbation and the projection; the lift exact
under DR and DR*, on a common noise path DR's run within the second order of MR's, and the regressor's point in the
law; DR's and DR*'s noise forces least-squares, their pointwise bounds on $g$, MR's $F_1$ three orders of magnitude
above; the quasi-static forces against the full second-order mean's noise part, the parameters' part above 1\% for DR
and DR* and below $10^{-5}$ for MR).}

% Numerics of Proposition D'''.31 (PARADIGM.md section 5d; the numbers are PARADIGM's copies of
% results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md).
\begin{sloppypar}
\paragraph{Numerics} (\code{exp_eiv_remedy.py} $\to$ \code{results/chaos/eiv_remedy.md}). The instance of
\hyperref[res:thmDppp:30]{Proposition D$'''$.30} with an Ornstein--Uhlenbeck noise of rate $B$; MR the measured
regressor and compensation, DR the regressor at the reference, DR* both at the reference. (A) The averaged system's
biases per unit $(\E\nu^2)^2$. From the noise's own moments, the adaptive prior's function-level bias at $k_e = 25$,
$B = 100$ is 2397 (MR), 0.471 (DR) and 0.0526 (DR*); with the parameters' fluctuation (the full second-order mean)
2398, 0.486 ($+3.3\%$) and 0.0481 ($-8.7\%$), the mean function's 2398, 0.476 and 0.0511. Over $k_e = 25$--$200$ and
$B = 100$, $1000$ the parameters' part changes MR's bias by at most 0.03\%, DR's by $-0.75\%$ to $+3.3\%$ except at
$k_e = B = 100$ --- where DR's noise part nearly cancels ($4.5\cdot10^{-5}$) and the parameters' part raises it by
